# HFEDM Engineering Specification

This specification is intended for readers who have studied the HFEDM theory and are preparing to implement or extend the model. It consolidates the mathematical models, states, inputs, parameters, and interfaces required for implementation.

For an initial implementation, use Sections 1–8 to establish semantics and inputs, Sections 9–13 to implement state updates, and Sections 15–16 for outputs and computation. Consult Section 14 for unit conversions and Section 17 for memory integration. Engineering defaults are collected in Section 12; they do not supersede the missing-value and provenance rules for individual input fields.

## 1 Scope and Module Responsibilities

<a id="sec-1-1"></a>

### 1.1 Engineering Definitions and Application

The HFEDM theory and this specification describe the same model, providing the theoretical account and the engineering definitions, respectively. The body and appendices of this document are the sole engineering specification for HFEDM implementation, interface integration, and extensions.

The body defines states, inputs, continuous equations, event maps, parameters, outputs, and intermodule contracts. The appendices provide mathematical properties, their applicability conditions, numerical criteria, and data-compatibility rules; they apply together with the body. Implementations must use the definitions, parameter domains, and update order explicitly specified here.

The specific functions, coefficients, and engineering initial values have not been calibrated against human data. Mathematical guarantees hold only under the stated conditions. The complete memory theory has not yet been integrated, and the real-world time interface has not been calibrated. This document does not additionally supply test suites, numerical acceptance thresholds, or procedures for human validation and calibration.

<a id="sec-1-2"></a>

### 1.2 Modeled Subject

The subject is a conversational agent with task goals, memory of objects, and a sustained-attention interface. Four categories of quantities must be distinguished:

1. Emotion expressed by the user: the observed or inferred user state.
2. Textual sentiment polarity: the positive or negative evaluation expressed by an utterance.
3. Task facts: progress, risk, success or failure, and outcome quality.
4. Emotion of the modeled subject: the state produced by the subject's appraisal of the above information.

The user's emotion is not copied directly into the subject's emotion. Negative wording also does not directly imply that the subject has been harmed.

<a id="sec-1-3"></a>

### 1.3 Modeling Scope

The model simulates emotion through mathematical states. It deliberately excludes subsystems for physiological arousal, hormones, endocrine processes, medication, sleep, and circadian rhythms. Sleep deprivation is not an equivalent definition of fatigue in this model.

Included:

- Subjective mental fatigue without a physiological subsystem: it accumulates through actual exertion and decreases according to remaining activity capacity and recovery opportunity. It is not equivalent to sleepiness, low mood, or insufficient motivation.
- Replay and recall generated by the memory module under specified conditions, including instantaneous replay and continuous recall intervals.
- Bounded, slow, experience-gated affective-style adaptation, constituting the limited personality evolution assigned to this module. Its magnitude is constrained by the specified parameter domain and does not represent a calibrated level of human subjective change.
- Sustained attention, tasks, and autonomous exploration. Even when the user is absent, permitted autonomous activity that actually occurs still contributes its emotional and fatigue effects and the effects of eligible experience on personality adaptation.

Excluded: very long-term physiological or psychological development, developmental changes, personality changes following disasters or trauma, clinical emotional disorders, the complete structure of human personality, and the full range of human emotions and cultural semantics. Dreams are outside the scope of this specification.

“Suspending active processing” is a software/activity mode, not biological sleep. It stops active inputs and personality learning, but does not stop emotional recovery in abstract time. The memory module remains responsible for its internal theories of selection, persistence, and reconsolidation. This specification defines the integration interface; it does not claim that integration of the two modules is complete.

<a id="sec-1-4"></a>

### 1.4 Personality Adaptation and Update Permissions

“Personality evolution” and “personality learning” here refer only to adaptation of emotional dispositions and emotional expression arising from emotion outputs. They do not authorize evolution of memory or other modules. L1 learning changes the emotional baseline \(b\); L2 sentiments learning changes object-directed dispositions within the emotion module. Neither updates external memory, knowledge, skills, or factual reliability.

L1's \(Q_E,P^E\) is a numerical buffer for learning emotional dispositions, holding the amount awaiting integration and the weighted sums over the ten emotion axes; \(\Xi_E,D_E\) provides its accounting. Capacity replacement, discarding, and fading only reduce the influence of some experiences on subsequent \(b\). They do not delete, forget, rewrite, or reindex the original memories. The buffer is not a separate event-content storage and retrieval module.

Personality learning must not modify memory content, weights, capacity, forgetting rates, retrieval rules, replay-selection rules, or reconsolidation policies. Nor may it modify task facts, permissions, tool policies, or parameters of other modules. Any future learnable response characteristics on the emotion side remain subject to the same ownership boundary; this specification does not enable them. The intermodule contracts in [17.1](#sec-17-1), [17.12–17.14](#sec-17-12), and [Appendices F.2–F.3](#sec-f-2) distinguish emotion-update authority from memory-recording authority. Memory stores emotion values and time through the encapsulation interface. New emotion readings after personality changes may become new memory data; existing historical snapshots are not rewritten as a result.

There are currently no independent, learnable expression-policy parameters. L1 changes emotion outputs through \(b\to m\to(e,w,x)\to y\), and downstream components may express emotion through the specified interface. Permission to read \(y\) does not authorize a downstream component to evolve general decision policies or other modules. Fatigue values also enter the expression interface directly, but this does not authorize downstream evolution of general capabilities, tool permissions, or memory policies. Normal storage of emotion data by memory follows [Section 17](#sec-17). The learning states remain limited to the emotional dispositions described above; fatigue itself is not stored as a permanent personality trait.

<a id="sec-1-5"></a>

### 1.5 Learning Types and Rate Boundaries

- Knowledge, memory, and understanding: encoding original content, retrieval, knowledge updates, skills, and problem-solving performance belong to external modules. This specification does not define their learning rates.
- L2 object-directed affective-disposition learning: updates the emotional evaluation of an object; it does not imply better factual understanding of that object.
- L1 affective-style integration: slowly changes the emotional baseline \(b\) using complete emotional-experience profiles; it does not imply faster memory learning.

A single negative or positive episode, fatigue, or “many failures” does not directly accelerate the integration rate. Failure acts only through verified events, persistent consequences, profiles covering all axes, and effective experience amounts. This specification does not use these to define general knowledge or memory learning rates.

<a id="sec-2"></a>

## 2 Five-Layer Architecture and State Responsibilities

The system has five functional layers. Fatigue is an auxiliary state and output of L5; sustained attention is an input and activity interface; the memory module is an external cooperating module. None adds another emotional functional layer.

| Layer | Name | States / parameters | Distinct function | Relative timescale |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| L1 | Temperament anchoring and affective-style adaptation | Fixed \(b_0,g,\theta_{\rm reg}\); slowly varying \(b\) and summary \(Q_E,P^E,\Xi_E,D_E\) | Stable individual differences and limited experience-based adaptation; no records of specific objects | Slowest; \(b\) remains fixed without actual experience integration |
| L2 | Object-directed sentiments | \(s_o^+,s_o^-\) | Retains affective dispositions toward particular people, groups, things, and activities | Slow; retention without passive forgetting is possible |
| L3 | Long-term affective background | \(m\) | Integrates the background and defines the admissible short-term space | Slow |
| L4 | Proximal event response | \(e\) | Proximal responses to new stimuli, reappraisal, ongoing situations, and recall | Fast |
| L5 | Short-term overall mood and regulation load | \(w,x,f\) | Constrained current mood; subjective fatigue, recovery, and regulation efficacy | Short-to-medium term for mood; independent load timescale for fatigue |

\(w\) is the coordinate of \(x\) within the moving constraint box. \(f\) is a mathematical proxy for subjective mental fatigue, not a claimed equivalent of any particular measurement scale. \(w\) is not separately output; \(f\) is output as the eleventh, auxiliary axis. It is neither a sixth layer nor an eleventh discrete emotion.

<a id="sec-2-1"></a>

### 2.1 Fixed Temperament and Adaptive Baseline

The fixed temperament anchor and the adaptive baseline are stored separately. \(b(\tau)\) represents only slow adaptation of the affective baseline; \(b_0,g,\theta_{\rm reg}\) remain fixed. \(b\) does not represent the Big Five, complete personality, or a calibrated personality scale.

<a id="sec-2-2"></a>

### 2.2 Interlayer Dependencies

\[
s_o\to\mathrm{appraisal}\to e\to m,\qquad b\to m,\qquad m\to e,
\]
\[
(e,m)\to w\to x,\qquad m\to\mathcal C(m),
\]
\[
\text{actual activity/regulation effort, recovery opportunity}\to f
\to(\text{regulation efficacy, compensatory effort, fatigue output})\to(e,y_{\rm Fat}).
\]

Eligible experience retains a profile of the \(e\) actually experienced and affects \(b\) through a summary covering all axes, capacity and discard rules, and integration fractions. It does not allow \(m\) to rewrite personality automatically. Direct evidence about an object updates sentiments. Memory replay can affect \(e,m,x,f\), but the reappearance of an old memory does not automatically create new object evidence. An eligible new reflective conclusion may affect \(b\) through a separate, explicit learning interface; replay alone does not automatically count as new experience.

The fixed \(g,\theta_{\rm reg},b_0\) receive neither short-term nor personality-learning feedback. This specification adapts the affective baseline \(b\); it does not implement learnable response gains or recovery rates. \(x\) does not rewrite task facts or serve directly as evidence for sentiments. The effect of \(e\) on \(b\) must pass through an eligible experience record. Without eligibility as new experience, residual mood cannot be repackaged as a new personality-learning allowance.

<a id="sec-2-3"></a>

### 2.3 State Semantics

- Sentiments are object-directed and are not currently occurring emotions.
- \(m\) is a background spanning events, not liking or disliking a particular object.
- \(e\) is a proximal response, not external evidence.
- \(x\) is the ten-axis mood state supplied to downstream models; the specified auxiliary fatigue output is separate.
- \(b\) adjusts the medium-to-long-term baseline; it does not replace \(m\)'s responsibility for spatial constraints.
- \(f\) is a proxy for subjective fatigue that accumulates and recovers, distinct from current effort, motivation, and objective performance. It is not an empirically established stock of “willpower fuel.”

Competition among attention sources and disengagement from residual responses are implemented in L4; no sixth layer is introduced. Fading of the L1 summary and retention of the learned \(b\) are distinct responsibilities; neither is deletion of external memory.

<a id="sec-3"></a>

## 3 Emotion Axes and Output Domain

Define the fixed axis order:

\[
\mathcal I=
(J,I,C,R,F,A,S,D,H,G).
\]

| Axis | Name | Psychological meaning in this specification | Meaning of 0 | Meaning of 1 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| \(J\) | Joy | A pleasurable response to favorable outcomes, gains, or satisfaction | No such response | Maximum joy intensity on this scale |
| \(I\) | Interest | Exploratory emotion toward comprehensible novelty or complexity | No exploratory interest | Maximum interest intensity on this scale |
| \(C\) | Concern / compassion | Concern for another's distress and an affective orientation toward alleviating it | No such concern | Maximum concern intensity on this scale |
| \(R\) | Relief | Emotion elicited by reduction or removal of a preceding threat | No relief response | Maximum relief intensity on this scale |
| \(F\) | Fear / anxiety | Defensive emotion toward threat, potential harm, or uncertain danger | No such defensive emotion | Maximum intensity on this scale |
| \(A\) | Anger | Antagonistic emotion toward obstruction, harm, or blameworthy negative outcomes | No anger | Maximum anger intensity on this scale |
| \(S\) | Sadness | Emotion concerning loss, deprivation, or outcomes difficult to reverse | No sadness | Maximum sadness intensity on this scale |
| \(D\) | Disgust | Rejective emotion toward contamination, aversive content, or an explicit object of disgust | No disgust | Maximum disgust intensity on this scale |
| \(H\) | Shame | Emotion concerning negative evaluation of the self as a whole | No shame | Maximum shame intensity on this scale |
| \(G\) | Guilt | Negative self-conscious emotion concerning one's own conduct, responsibility, or norm violations | No guilt | Maximum guilt intensity on this scale |

All ten axes have the same direction: a larger value means stronger emotion of that type. An auxiliary axis is also included:

| Axis | Name | Psychological meaning | Meaning of 0 | Meaning of 1 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| \(\mathrm{Fat}\) | Subjective mental fatigue | State proxy \(f\) for the modeled subject's sense that mental activity is effortful or tiring | No accumulated mental fatigue in this model; not equivalent to high vitality | Maximum fatigue on this scale; not equivalent to incapacity, inevitable failure, or a need for sleep |

\(\mathrm{Fat}\) is computed from the actual effort history and recovery equation, not from the ten-axis appraisal mapping. \(f\) is represented separately from interest, low mood, current effort, motivation, and objective performance.

The final axis order is \((J,I,C,R,F,A,S,D,H,G,\mathrm{Fat})\), comprising eleven values. The ten-dimensional geometry, personality profiles, and appraisal below still use only indices \(i\in\mathcal I\). \(\mathrm{Fat}\) must not be automatically included in personality learning or the emotion constraint box. The complete output domain is \(\mathcal C(m)\times[0,1]\): all short-term mood axes remain constrained by the long-term background, while the fatigue domain is maintained by its load equation.

These values:

- Are not event probabilities.
- Are not confidence scores for emotion classification.
- Are not required to sum to \(1\).
- Are not assumed statistically independent or psychologically orthogonal.
- Do not imply that identical values across people or cultures represent equal subjective intensity.

<a id="sec-3-1"></a>

### 3.1 Distinctions Between Axes

Retain all ten axes and allow multiple axes to be nonzero simultaneously. Implementations must distinguish threat from blame attribution, realized loss from anticipated threat, evaluation of the whole self from responsibility for conduct, gain from threat removal, exploratory understanding from enjoyment of outcomes, and another's distress from the subject's own sadness. Outputs must not be converted into PAD or mutually exclusive classifications.

<a id="sec-3-2"></a>

### 3.2 Representational Scope

This specification does not separately output surprise, awe, pride, gratitude, romantic love, jealousy, boredom, calmness, or similar states.

Furthermore:

- Fear and anxiety are combined, losing distinctions in temporal orientation and certainty.
- Task frustration is partly mapped to anger or sadness, without a separate axis.
- Positive sentiments are not equivalent to love and do not constitute a complete attachment model.
- Outputs do not retain the object, cause, or appraisal uncertainty of each emotion.

The ten dimensions are a limited representation preserving selected important distinctions for this application. They are not a complete space of human emotions.

<a id="sec-3-3"></a>

### 3.3 Scale Endpoints and Reachable Upper Bounds

0 and 1 are theoretical endpoints of each axis's scale; reachability is not guaranteed for every parameter setting, initial condition, or history. At any time, the reachable range is first constrained by \(\mathcal C(m)\). If \(b_i\le b_i^{\max}\) and \(m_i(0)\le M_i\), then
\[
m_i(\tau)\le\max\{M_i,(1-\kappa)b_i^{\max}+\kappa\},
\quad
x_i\le1-\ell_i+\ell_i\max\{M_i,(1-\kappa)b_i^{\max}+\kappa\}.
\]
This is a conditional upper bound; an equilibrium value is not directly treated as an all-time bound for arbitrary initial conditions. Axes must not be dynamically renormalized merely to make the numbers appear to fill the 0–1 range. This specification does not use \((-1,1)\) outputs, and absent emotions need not be mapped to negative values.

No calibration has been established between output numbers and human labels such as “mild,” “moderate,” and “strong.” A peak range from a single event or initial condition cannot assign severity labels to all emotions. Outputs must not be dynamically stretched to match downstream verbal labels.

### Local Symbol Index

Symbols with the same written form are interpreted according to their local definitions in the relevant section.

| Symbol | Location and meaning |
|---|---|
| \(t\) | Threat appraisal in Sections 6–7, also denoted \(t_{\mathrm{thr}}\); \(t,s\) in Section 9.3.3 are abstract-time values; in Section 17, shared elapsed real-world time |
| \(g_i\) and \(g^\pm\) | Temperament gain and gain/harm appraisal, respectively |
| \(f\) and \(f_j\) | Subjective fatigue and the task-closure indicator, respectively |
| \(P_j\), \(P_i(e,m)\), \(P_i^E\) | Task progress, constraint-box projection, and L1 emotion-weighted sums, respectively |
| \(a_i\) | Appraisal mapping in Section 7; local rate in the frozen stepping method in Section 16.2 |
| \(\theta\) | Event-flow coordinate in Section 11; a parameter placeholder for a future classification threshold in Section 17.14; other local analyses use their own definitions |
| \(\theta_{\rm reg}\), \(\theta_i^r\) | Fixed temperament-regulation parameter and down-regulation threshold, respectively |


<a id="sec-4"></a>

## 4 Time, Events, and Continuous Inputs

<a id="sec-4-1"></a>

### 4.1 Abstract Time

Define:

\[
\tau\in[0,\infty),\qquad
\Delta\tau=\tau_2-\tau_1\ge0.
\]

\(\tau\) is an independent variable, not a count of conversation turns, messages, tokens, or task steps.

At this stage, only positions and intervals in abstract time are used; real-clock data are not connected.

<a id="sec-4-2"></a>

### 4.2 Real-World Time Interface

The following linear real-world time interface is reserved for later calibration:

\[
\Delta\tau=\frac{\Delta t_{\rm real}}{T_{\rm ref}},
\qquad T_{\rm ref}>0.
\]

where:

- \(T_{\rm ref}\): unit conversion between real-world time and abstract time.
- \(\lambda_e,\lambda_x,\lambda_m,\lambda_s\): a dynamical rate in abstract time.
- \(1/\lambda\): the abstract-time constant of the corresponding equation.

For a pure first-order recovery term, the real-world time constant is:

\[
T_{\rm real}=\frac{T_{\rm ref}}{\lambda}.
\]

This specification assigns no real-world numerical value to \(T_{\rm ref}\). In subsequent normal integration with the memory module, both modules use the same elapsed real-world time \(t=t_{\rm real}\), time unit, and effective event-time semantics. Emotion-local time is also denoted \(\tau_E=\tau\) and \(T_E=T_{\rm ref}\); the memory-local timescale may differ. [17.6–17.14](#sec-17-6) and [Appendices F.1–F.3](#sec-f-1) provide compatibility conditions for time, encapsulation, shared definitions, and use of readings. Sharing \(t\) does not make the recovery rates of different layers identical.

<a id="sec-4-3"></a>

### 4.3 Three Sources of Influence

1. **One-time events**
   \[
   E_k\text{ occurs at }\tau_k.
   \]

2. **Continuous inputs**
   \[
   U_j\text{ acts on }[a_j,b_j).
   \]

3. **Internal residual state**
   States already present in \(e,m,s_o,w\) continue to evolve according to the equations.

A single event executes exactly one jump. Its aftereffects persist in the state and must not be disguised as a continuing stream of new events.

Intervals between consecutive events:

\[
\Delta\tau_k=\tau_k-\tau_{k-1}
\]

may be arbitrarily unequal.

<a id="sec-4-4"></a>

### 4.4 Admissible Input Sequences

- Every finite abstract-time interval contains finitely many events; Zeno sequences with infinitely many events in finite time are excluded.
- Simultaneous events form a semantically ordered batch, processed according to [Section 11](#sec-11). Ordering does not add psychological time.
- Continuous inputs are bounded, piecewise-continuous functions. More generally, bounded measurable inputs are allowed with Carathéodory solutions.
- Each model instance uses finite sets of objects and task goals. Experience, memory, and concern records may be added at locally finite event times.
- Input identifiers must distinguish new evidence from retransmitted data.
- Event doses and continuous-exposure intensities must not be determined directly by message counts or solver-step counts.
- When the memory module is connected, local finiteness and causal ordering must hold for the complete coupled event stream, as specified in [17.8](#sec-17-8). Separate compliance by the two modules does not replace the joint conditions.

<a id="sec-4-5"></a>

### 4.5 Distinguishing Event-Dose Progress from Time

\(\sigma\) in [Section 11](#sec-11) parameterizes only the update amount for one event; it is not psychological time. Experience amounts, message chunks, replay counts, and integration steps do not replace \(\tau\). Attention-mode changes do not stop \(\tau\) either. All transformations of time-dependent parameters follow [Section 14](#sec-14).

<a id="sec-5"></a>

## 5 Input Quantification Interface

<a id="sec-5-1"></a>

### 5.1 Separating Observations, Inferences, and Emotional States

Each linguistically inferred input stores:

\[
(\widehat v,q_v,\mathrm{source},\mathrm{target},\mathrm{status}),
\]

where:

- \(\widehat v\): encoded content value.
- \(q_v\in[0,1]\): evidence-reliability weight for this encoding.
- \(\mathrm{source}\): identifier of the source content or task record.
- \(\mathrm{target}\): the referenced object.
- \(\mathrm{status}\): directly observed, reported by the person concerned, inferred, or missing.

\(q_v\) is not a calibrated probability of correctness. Treating it as a probability requires a separately established measurement model.

<a id="sec-5-2"></a>

### 5.2 Encoding Natural-Language Intensity

This specification uses the following engineering anchors:

\[
\widehat v\in\{0,0.25,0.5,0.75,1\}.
\]

They denote, respectively: not expressed, weak, moderate, strong, and explicitly very strong.

The default reliability anchors are:

| \(q_v\) | Encoding meaning |
|---:|---|
| 0 | Missing or without usable evidence |
| 0.25 | Indirect, ambiguous, and open to multiple interpretations |
| 0.5 | Supported by context but still requiring inference |
| 0.75 | Explicitly expressed, but the facts or referent remain incompletely confirmed |
| 1 | Directly determinable under the operational definition of the field |

For example, whether text explicitly expresses a negative evaluation may be directly determinable, while whether the speaker is actually angry may remain an inference. The two cannot share the same justification for reliability.

Mapping raw text to these encodings belongs to this module's **measurement interface**. This specification does not claim to establish a uniquely correct interpretation of natural language. Once valid numerical encodings are supplied, downstream computation is fully deterministic.

<a id="sec-5-3"></a>

### 5.3 Conversational Polarity

Define:

\[
p^+,p^-\in[0,1].
\]

These denote positive and negative textual-evaluation intensities, respectively, and may both be nonzero. If a conventional scalar polarity is needed, additionally compute:

\[
p=p^+-p^-\in[-1,1],
\]

The two channels are nevertheless retained internally.

Also define:

- \(d_{\rm self}\in[0,1]\): direct relevance of the content to the subject.
- \(h^+,h^-\in[0,1]\): whether the content constitutes support for or harm to the subject.
- \(q_p\in[0,1]\): combined reliability of the polarity and its target.

Social gain and harm encodings:

\[
g_{\rm chat}^+
=d_{\rm self}q_p p^+h^+,
\]

\[
g_{\rm chat}^-
=d_{\rm self}q_p p^-h^-.
\]

Thus, a negative statement that merely describes the user's own distress does not automatically produce an appraisal that the subject has been harmed.

<a id="sec-5-4"></a>

### 5.4 Task Progress and Outcomes

Progress of task \(j\) is defined as:

\[
P_j=\sum_{r=1}^{M_j}\omega_{jr}c_{jr},
\]

\[
\omega_{jr}\ge0,\qquad
\sum_r\omega_{jr}=1,\qquad
c_{jr}\in[0,1].
\]

\(\omega_{jr}\) is a predefined workload or outcome weight. It must not be arbitrarily increased after completion to manufacture positive emotion.

Outcome quality:

\[
Q_j=\sum_{\ell=1}^{K_j}v_{j\ell}
\,\operatorname{clip}_{[0,1]}
\left(
\frac{r_{j\ell}-L_{j\ell}}
{U_{j\ell}-L_{j\ell}}
\right),
\]

where:

\[
v_{j\ell}\ge0,\quad
\sum_\ell v_{j\ell}=1,\quad
U_{j\ell}>L_{j\ell}.
\]

For indicators where lower is better, use one minus the normalized value above.

Task status is:

\[
\mathrm{status}_j\in
\{\text{not started, in progress, successful, partially completed, failed, cancelled}\}.
\]

Status labels do not directly correspond to emotions. In particular, cancellation does not automatically mean failure, and success does not automatically mean that every outcome-quality value is \(1\).

Define the task-value estimate:

\[
V_j=
(1-f_j)
\left[
\omega_P P_j+(1-\omega_P)\widehat Q_j
\right]
+f_jQ_j,
\]

where:

- \(f_j=0\): not yet closed.
- \(f_j=1\): closed.
- \(\widehat Q_j\in[0,1]\): the current estimate of final outcome quality.
- \(\omega_P\in[0,1]\): the engineering weight of progress in the value estimate.

The value change for one task update is:

\[
\Delta V_j=V_j^{\rm after}-V_j^{\rm before}.
\]

Using the subject's goal weight \(\pi_j\ge0,\sum_j\pi_j=1\), define:

\[
g_{\rm task}^+
=\sum_j\pi_j[\Delta V_j]_+,
\qquad
g_{\rm task}^-
=\sum_j\pi_j[-\Delta V_j]_+.
\]

where \([a]_+=\max(a,0)\).

The same update must not additionally sum \(\Delta P_j\), the success label, and \(\Delta V_j\) as separate contributions; doing so would count the same outcome repeatedly.

Continuous task inputs separately describe obstacles, risks, burdens, and favorable conditions that **still exist now**. They do not resubmit past \(\Delta V_j\).

<a id="sec-5-5"></a>

### 5.5 Fields Required for Appraisal

All of the following are inputs representing the subject's appraisal of an event, not emotion outputs:

| Symbol | Domain | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| \(r_{\rm rel}\) | \([0,1]\) | Relevance to the subject's goals or concerns |
| \(q_{\rm evt}\) | \([0,1]\) | Reliability of the event's existence and availability |
| \(\widehat g^+,\widehat g^-\) | \([0,1]\) | Gain and harm before incorporating sentiments |
| \(\widehat t\) | \([0,1]\) | Threat level |
| \(u\) | \([0,1]\) | Outcome uncertainty |
| \(c\) | \([0,1]\) | Controllability: the subject's ability to change the outcome |
| \(k\) | \([0,1]\) | Comprehensibility and manageability |
| \(n\) | \([0,1]\) | Novelty / complexity |
| \(b_{\rm obs}\) | \([0,1]\) | Degree of goal obstruction |
| \(a_{\rm other},a_{\rm self}\) | \([0,1]\) | Responsibility attributed to others and to oneself |
| \(l\) | \([0,1]\) | Magnitude of realized loss |
| \(i_{\rm rev}\) | \([0,1]\) | Irrecoverability of the loss |
| \(d_{\rm rej}\) | \([0,1]\) | Appraisal of contamination / aversive content |
| \(h_{\rm self}\) | \([0,1]\) | Negative evaluation of the whole self |
| \(v_{\rm norm}\) | \([0,1]\) | Degree of norm violation by one's own conduct |
| \(z_{\rm other}\) | \([0,1]\) | Degree of another's distress |
| \(h_{\rm other}\) | \([0,1]\) | Harm to others caused by one's own conduct |
| \(t_{\rm before},t_{\rm after}\) | \([0,1]\) | Appraisal of the same threat before and after a change |
| \(q_\Delta\) | \([0,1]\) | Reliability of the determination that the threat changed |

Gain and harm may be formed as:

\[
\widehat g^\pm
=\operatorname{clip}_{[0,1]}
\left(
g_{\rm task}^\pm+
\omega_{\rm soc}g_{\rm chat}^\pm
\right)
\]

where \(\omega_{\rm soc}\in[0,1]\).

For a threat arising from quantifiable task risk, one may use:

\[
\widehat t
=\operatorname{clip}_{[0,1]}
\left(
\sum_j\pi_j
\,\mathrm{severity}_j
\,\widehat{\Pr}_j(\mathrm{adverse})
\right).
\]

Only \(\widehat{\Pr}_j\) is a probability-type input here; the emotion axes remain nonprobabilistic.

<a id="sec-5-6"></a>

### 5.6 Masks, Source Semantics, and Task-Loss Completion

Missing data are stored as NA with zero reliability; they are not equivalent to a confirmed intensity of zero. Each axis requires applicability, scope, the source of the determination, and \(M_{ri}\). Set \(M_{ri}=1\) only when applicability can be determined and sufficient observations or explicitly identified inferences are available.

| Axis | Primary evidence required |
|---|---|
| J | Favorable outcome / gain |
| I | Novelty / complexity and comprehensibility |
| C | Another's distress |
| R | Before-and-after change in the same threat and evidence of its removal |
| F | Threat content, or a threat inference with an explicit contextual basis |
| A | Harm / obstruction and attribution information |
| S | Loss already realized or confirmed at closure |
| D | Explicitly aversive / contaminating content |
| H | Negative evaluation of the whole self |
| G | Norm violation by one's own conduct and one's own responsibility |

Unevaluated axes do not undergo projection updates. If the primary information is known and only secondary modifiers are missing, explicit engineering defaults may be used: control, comprehension, and uncertainty take 0.5, while responsibility is not automatically assigned to a person. These defaults remain marked as inferred and do not create direct evidence. Completion through sentiments requires a relevant context; liking or disliking an object cannot by itself enable every axis mask.

All intensities are operational appraisals, not probabilities. Reliability and the source of each determination are stored separately. Appraisal bias, data confidence, and actual emotion cannot substitute for one another.

At task closure, retain the expected quality immediately before closure, \(Q_j^{\rm exp}\), and the current outcome, \(Q_j\). Let \(c_j^{\rm close}\in\{0,1\}\) indicate whether this is the first valid closure evidence, and let \(q_j^{\rm result}\in[0,1]\) denote outcome reliability:

\[
l_{\rm task}=\operatorname{clip}_{[0,1]}
\sum_j\pi_jc_j^{\rm close}q_j^{\rm result}[Q_j^{\rm exp}-Q_j]_+.
\]

Let \(l_{\rm other}\in[0,1]\) denote other losses encoded using the anchors in [5.2](#sec-5-2), excluding any duplication of this task outcome, and define \(l=\operatorname{clip}_{[0,1]}(l_{\rm task}+l_{\rm other})\). Keep everything in the same appraisal record; do not create a second failure event with the same dose. Identify tasks by goal_id and attempt_version. Retransmitting closure for the same attempt must not set \(c_j^{\rm close}=1\) again.

Encode \(i_{\rm rev}\) using the intensity anchors in [5.2](#sec-5-2): 0 means known to be fully recoverable, 1 means known to be irrecoverable, and intermediate values mean partially recoverable. Evidence includes retryability, remaining resources, and reversibility of the outcome. If unknown, retain NA. In an S-axis mapping where a loss has been confirmed, 0 may temporarily be used as a conservative modifier, but must not be recorded as “confirmed recoverable.”

\(g^-\) and \(l\) may describe different aspects of the same event and affect different emotion axes; their shared event dose is counted only once. An outcome below expectations does not automatically imply immorality, failure of the whole self, or blameworthiness of others. It therefore does not automatically enable H, G, or attribution of blame to others.

Changes in progress, outcome quality, and expectations remain subject to the single value update in [5.4](#sec-5-4); no additional gain is added for the same progress. Continuous sources describe conditions that remain present and must not resubmit an old \(\Delta V\) at every solver step.

<a id="sec-5-7"></a>

### 5.7 Canonical Events and the Evidence-Amount Contract

An event is an independently determinable change in semantic facts, not a message, sentence, or token. The input adapter produces:
\[
(\mathrm{event\_id},\mathrm{actor},\mathrm{target},
\mathrm{predicate},\mathrm{occurrence\_id},
\mathrm{attempt\_version},\mathrm{evidence\_ids},\mathrm{finalized},\mathrm{order}).
\]

Supplementary descriptions of the same action, task attempt, and outcome change share an occurrence_id. Restatements, translations, summaries, and transmission chunks do not create new events. An action that actually occurs again receives a new occurrence_id even if the wording is identical; this distinction follows factual records, not string differences or a fixed elapsed time. Independent concurrent facts may be separate events, but events at the same time are still processed jointly under [Section 11](#sec-11). Without sufficient evidence to distinguish events, retain one event and mark the uncertainty; additional splitting must not amplify the response.

Each event's evidence_ids correspond to a deduplicated finite set of semantic propositions \(\mathcal E_r\). Assign each proposition an information weight \(\zeta_{rj}\in[0,1]\): 0 for known duplication or no new content; 0.25 for a limited addition; 0.5 for a new, independently verifiable detail; and 1 for a new core fact. 0.75 is allowed as an intermediate anchor. These weights are not emotion intensity, reliability, or word count. A paraphrase of the same proposition is still counted only once. Define raw information amount and accepted dose as:
\[
d_r^{\rm raw}=\sum_{j\in\mathcal E_r}\zeta_{rj},\qquad
S_d(d)=d_{\rm sat}(1-e^{-d/d_{\rm sat}}),\quad d_{\rm sat}>0.
\]
\[
d_r=S_d(d_r^{\rm raw}),\qquad
\xi_r=d_rq_{{\rm evt},r}r_{{\rm rel},r}.
\]

Reliability and relevance remain separate. This formula does not claim that all three are psychological scales identifiable from a single output. Merge the same evidence set before applying saturation; applying saturation separately to fragments and then adding them is prohibited.

By default, exactly one jump is committed only when finalized=true. Before commitment, fragments update pending data only and do not generate new emotions per fragment; existing states continue evolving normally while waiting. A gradual response at distinct abstract times requires independently valid increments of new facts, linked as new events to the same cause, with evidence_ids explicitly identifying what is new relative to the original event. Arbitrary text fragments must not be treated as new facts merely to support streaming.

An event's \(\tau_k\) is the abstract-time position at which the subject acquires the evidence eligible for commitment. The occurrence time of a historical fact is stored separately as content; late-arriving data are not inserted into an already committed trajectory to rewrite it retrospectively. Corrections are submitted as correction events at a new abstract time, preserving the relationships between the preceding and subsequent evidence.

Memory replay is not new external information. It receives a separately authorized replay dose under [Section 17](#sec-17), still subject to the same single-event saturation function and total budget. Different source semantics must not be conflated. Mapping natural language to canonical events still requires an external measurement interface. This specification makes numerical computation deterministic once valid event records are supplied; it does not claim to uniquely recover actual psychological events from arbitrary text.

<a id="sec-5-8"></a>

### 5.8 Activity and Fatigue Input Contract

[Section 9](#sec-9) supplements continuous inputs with activity mode, \(u_{\rm task},u_{\rm rec}\), attention sources and cause scopes, regulation targets / complete effort overrides, \(q_{\rm rest},\chi,g_E\), complete experience intervals, participation masks, and fractions. Each item requires an effective abstract-time interval. Regulation effort additionally retains its provenance label under [9.3.4](#sec-9-3-4). Only explicitly specified engineering rules may supply defaults; missing data must not silently be treated as observed zeros.

Activity workload, current effort, fatigue \(f\), the subject's emotion, and task performance are distinct quantities. A task interface may report high demand but low actual exertion; the fatigue equation uses actual exertion. \(u_\Sigma>1\), negative effort, or quality outside its domain must be rejected or explicitly corrected upstream; the integrator must not silently redistribute it. The user's own statement of being “tired” is information about the user only. The subject's fatigue is determined by its own activity specified here and its history under [Section 10](#sec-10).

<a id="sec-6"></a>

## 6 Sentiments: Object Representation, Persistence, and Appraisal Effects

<a id="sec-6-1"></a>

### 6.1 Object Set

Fix the object set of a model instance:

\[
\mathcal O=\{o_1,\ldots,o_N\}.
\]

Each object has:

\[
o=(\mathrm{id},\mathrm{type},\mathrm{referent}),
\]

where type may be a person, group, thing, activity, or task type.

The input interface handles object identification and alias merging. Adding an object may be treated as extending the state space. New slots must be created according to the initialization rules; memory of an old object must not be implicitly reused.

For each object, store:

\[
s_o^+\in[0,1],\qquad s_o^-\in[0,1].
\]

Their respective meanings are:

- \(s_o^+\): positive affective disposition toward the object.
- \(s_o^-\): negative affective disposition toward the object.

Both may be high, representing ambivalence; they need not be complementary.

This is a limited operationalization of sentiments, not a complete account of the distinct relational structures of love, respect, attachment, hatred, and similar constructs.

<a id="sec-6-2"></a>

### 6.2 Object Weights for Events

Each event specifies:

\[
\omega_{ko}\ge0,\qquad
\sum_o\omega_{ko}\le1.
\]

Without sufficient evidence pointing to a particular object, task failure must not automatically be attributed to the conversational user.

Object dispositions activated by an event:

\[
\bar s_k^\pm=\sum_o\omega_{ko}s_o^\pm.
\]

<a id="sec-6-3"></a>

### 6.3 Effects of Sentiments on Ambiguous Appraisals

For relevant events or continuous object inputs, complete the gain, harm, and threat appraisals using the following equations.

\[
g^\pm
=\operatorname{clip}_{[0,1]}
\left(
q_g\widehat g^\pm+
\beta_s(1-q_g)\bar s^\pm
\right),
\]

\[
t
=\operatorname{clip}_{[0,1]}
\left(
q_t\widehat t+
\beta_s(1-q_t)\bar s^-
\right),
\]

where:

\[
q_g,q_t,\beta_s\in[0,1].
\]

Therefore:

- When evidence is complete, sentiments do not override explicit evidence for that field.
- When evidence is ambiguous, existing dispositions may supply part of the appraisal.
- Sentiments do not spontaneously generate new events without a relevant event or continuous object input.

The learning target for sentiments uses direct gain/harm evidence **before this bias is added**, avoiding a self-reinforcing loop.

<a id="sec-7"></a>

## 7 Appraisal and Mapping to Ten Axes

Processing order: event evidence and goal changes → relational appraisal across multiple fields → ten-axis response encoding.

<a id="sec-7-1"></a>

### 7.1 Response Targets Before Temperament Gain

Define \(r\in[0,1]^{10}\):

\[
r_J=g^+\left(1-\frac{t}{2}\right),
\]

\[
r_I=nk,
\]

\[
r_C=z_{\rm other}\left(\frac12+\frac c2\right),
\]

\[
r_R=q_\Delta[t_{\rm before}-t_{\rm after}]_+(1-u),
\]

\[
r_F=t\left(1-\frac c2\right)
\left(\frac12+\frac u2\right),
\]

\[
r_A=g^-
\left[
\frac14+\frac34\max(a_{\rm other},b_{\rm obs})
\right]
\left(\frac12+\frac c2\right),
\]

\[
r_S=l\left(\frac12+\frac{i_{\rm rev}}2\right),
\]

\[
r_D=d_{\rm rej},
\]

\[
r_H=h_{\rm self},
\]

\[
r_G=v_{\rm norm}a_{\rm self}
\left(\frac12+\frac{h_{\rm other}}2\right).
\]

Shame does not require public exposure. A public/private dichotomy must not replace the respective field definitions of shame and guilt.

<a id="sec-7-2"></a>

### 7.2 Temperament Gain

For each axis, specify:

\[
g_i\in[g_{\min},g_{\max}],
\qquad 0<g_{\min}\le g_{\max}<\infty.
\]

Define:

\[
\phi_g(r)=\frac{gr}{1+(g-1)r}.
\]

The mapping result is:

\[
a_i=\phi_{g_i}(r_i).
\]

This function satisfies:

\[
\phi_g(0)=0,\quad
\phi_g(1)=1,\quad
0\le\phi_g(r)\le1,
\]

\[
\frac{\partial\phi_g}{\partial r}
=\frac{g}{[1+(g-1)r]^2}>0.
\]

\(a\) is an appraisal encoding, not an emotion that has already occurred. For continuous inputs and reassess, it is a response target; for stimulate, it is the single-event relative increment defined in [Section 11](#sec-11). These modes must not be conflated. Only axes with enabled masks participate in the update.

Fix \(r\). Then
\[
\frac{\partial\phi_g(r)}{\partial g}
=\frac{r(1-r)}{[1+(g-1)r]^2}\ge0,\qquad
\frac{\partial\phi_g(r)}{\partial\log g}
=\phi_g(r)[1-\phi_g(r)]\le\frac14.
\]
and therefore
\[
|\phi_{g_2}(r)-\phi_{g_1}(r)|
\le\frac14|\log(g_2/g_1)|.
\]
More precisely, for \(0<g_1\le g_2\),
\[
\max_{0\le r\le1}[\phi_{g_2}(r)-\phi_{g_1}(r)]
=\frac{\sqrt{g_2}-\sqrt{g_1}}{\sqrt{g_2}+\sqrt{g_1}}
=\tanh\!\left(\frac14\log\frac{g_2}{g_1}\right).
\]
Let \(v=r/(1-r)\). The difference is
\((g_2-g_1)v/[(1+g_1v)(1+g_2v)]\), whose maximum occurs at \(v=1/\sqrt{g_1g_2}\); at the endpoints \(r=0,1\), the difference is zero. Thus, a relative change in gain is not an equally large absolute change in appraisal, nor does it guarantee a change in mood output rounded to three decimal places. The preceding logarithmic upper bound also follows directly using \(\tanh z\le z\) (\(z\ge0\)). This analysis does not enable learning of \(g\).

<a id="sec-7-3"></a>

### 7.3 Keeping Relevance Separate from Axis Semantics

Define the effective event weight:

\[
\xi_k=d_kq_{{\rm evt},k}r_{{\rm rel},k},
\qquad 0\le d_k\le d_{\rm sat}.
\]

\(d_k\) is a processing dose with no time unit. For a new external event, it represents effective new information. For memory replay, it is the separately authorized reprocessing-effect dose under [Section 17](#sec-17), without claiming that old memory becomes new evidence. Neither duplicates the encoding of emotion intensity or importance, and neither equals message count, token count, or elapsed time.

Without relevance or reliable event data, \(\xi_k=0\). A default for a missing field does not trigger an emotional jump.

<a id="sec-8"></a>

## 8 Moving Constraint Box

The long-term background determines the admissible region for short-term mood.

<a id="sec-8-1"></a>

### 8.1 Constraint Strength

For each axis, specify:

\[
0<\ell_{\min}\le \ell_i\le\ell_{\max}<1.
\]

A larger \(\ell_i\) means that the long-term background constrains the short-term state more strongly.

It may be set from the temperament-regulation parameter \(\theta_{\rm reg}\in[0,1]\):

\[
\ell_i=
\ell_{i,\rm low}
+
(\ell_{i,\rm high}-\ell_{i,\rm low})
\theta_{\rm reg}.
\]

This mapping is a geometric choice in this specification, not a conversion from an effortful-control scale.

The geometric definitions here apply together with the full parameter domain in [12.2](#sec-12-2) and the quantization-grid-spacing condition in [15.2](#sec-15-2).

<a id="sec-8-2"></a>

### 8.2 Admissible Region Determined by the Long-Term State

Define:

\[
L_i(m)=\ell_i m_i,
\]

\[
U_i(m)=\ell_i m_i+1-\ell_i.
\]

Short-term mood must lie in:

\[
\boxed{
\mathcal C(m)=
\prod_{i=1}^{10}[L_i(m),U_i(m)]
}
\]

Therefore:

\[
\ell_i m_i\le x_i\le
1-\ell_i(1-m_i).
\]

This directly constrains every admissible short-term state; it is not merely an adjustment to a decay rate or moving average.

For any event, regardless of appraisal strength:

\[
x_i\le U_i(m).
\]

For any situation that suddenly becomes positive:

\[
x_i\ge L_i(m).
\]

The latter means that a long-term negative background cannot disappear completely and immediately after one event. The former means that a normally low background limits sudden peaks.

<a id="sec-8-3"></a>

### 8.3 Region Center, Shape, and Strength

Center:

\[
c_i^{\mathcal C}(m)
=\ell_i m_i+\frac{1-\ell_i}{2}.
\]

Width:

\[
W_i=1-\ell_i.
\]

Volume:

\[
\operatorname{Vol}\mathcal C(m)
=\prod_i(1-\ell_i).
\]

The shape and width are fixed in this specification; the position moves with \(m\).

The emotion axes do not share a total intensity budget.

<a id="sec-8-4"></a>

### 8.4 Effects on the Output Range

The constraint box lowers instantaneous mood peaks under a low background and imposes a positive lower bound on mood axes with a positive background. These constraints are model behavior. Box boundaries must not be relaxed, and outputs must not be renormalized, merely to display stronger responses.

<a id="sec-9"></a>

## 9 Activity, Attention, Regulation, and Experience Eligibility

<a id="sec-9-1"></a>

### 9.1 Activity Modes

Mode \(z_{\rm mode}\) is piecewise constant: interaction, autonomous exploration, sustained-attention standby, or suspension of active processing. The first three accept tasks, concerns, and recall within their permissions. During complete suspension, attention, work, recall, regulation, integration effort, and new continuous experience registration are all zero, while passive evolution of emotion, fatigue, and unintegrated summaries continues. Suspension is not biological sleep.

The user's departure does not automatically change modes or directly produce sadness or personality learning. Autonomous exploration requires allowed_autonomy=true and actual activity; this specification does not itself authorize exploration.

<a id="sec-9-2"></a>

### 9.2 Attention Competition and Evidence-Based Disengagement

Each source supplies importance \(I_j\), reliability \(q_j^U\), and current effectiveness \(u_j^U\), all in \([0,1]\), with the encoding basis required by [5.2](#sec-5-2):
\[
p_j=I_jq_j^Uu_j^U,\qquad\bar v_j=v_{\max}p_j.
\]
Without an external override, nonsuspended modes use
\[
h_j=\begin{cases}p_j/\sum_kp_k,&\sum_kp_k>0,\\0,&\sum_kp_k=0.\end{cases}
\qquad v_j=h_j\bar v_j.
\]
An explicit attention controller may override the complete admissible vector, with \(\sum h_j\le1\). A single full-strength source can still reach \(\alpha=1\). A task that is actually being processed may occupy attention even without emotional content; use \(M=0\) for emotion axes that have not been appraised, rather than fabricating zero appraisals. Increasing work effort without changing attention sources must not be called distraction.

Also supply:

- \(\upsilon_j^{\rm attend}\in[0,1]\): the degree to which this activity actually absorbs attention, encoded by the anchors 0, .25, .5, .75, and 1 for none, light, moderate, high, and complete.
- \(c_{ji}^{\rm away}\in[0,1]\): the degree to which evidence supports the determination that the activity shifts attention away from emotional content on current axis \(i\).

\[
\boxed{d_i^{\rm away}=\sum_j h_j\upsilon_j^{\rm attend}c_{ji}^{\rm away}\in[0,1].}
\]
Because \(e\) is not decomposed by cause, a complete \(c^{\rm away}=1\) requires an assessment scope covering the currently known active and residual causes on that axis. Knowing only that an activity is unrelated to one cause does not justify applying that conclusion to the entire axis. Use zero when the source is the same, the content is still being processed, or evidence is insufficient. Partial attentional disengagement may use intermediate values marked as inferred. The external adapter retains cause_scope and supporting evidence. Without an override, an activity confirmed to be wholly unrelated and actually being processed takes \(c=1\); confirmed same-source or unknown cases take zero. \(\upsilon^{\rm attend}\) uses an engineering proxy based on that activity's actual effort fraction; it is not counted again as a second effort contribution.

This default proxy requires an effort allocation \(u_j^{\rm act}\ge0\) for each activity. Task allocations sum to no more than \(u_{\rm task}\), and recall allocations to no more than \(u_{\rm rec}\); duplicate sources must not receive another allocation. A single known activity may use all actual effort of its category. For multiple activities, the work interface supplies the allocation, or an explicit rule allocates it in proportion to existing attention weights within that category. If the allocation cannot be determined, use \(\upsilon^{\rm attend}=0\) and mark it unknown; total workload alone does not establish disengagement across an entire axis.

High workload does not imply attentional disengagement: the same demanding activity may be a threat source or may draw attention away from other content. \(h\) controls the drive from current sources; \(d^{\rm away}\) acts only on residual responses above \(T\) in [10.6](#sec-10-6). It does not subtract the same appraisal intensity a second time. The attentional effect of a neutral task does not require invented positive emotion.

The default effective \(v_j=v_{\max}p_j^2/\sum_kp_k\), extended by zero at the all-zero point, is Lipschitz. \(d^{\rm away}\) may be treated directly as a bounded input when supplied as an externally fixed time series. If its coefficients or the attention controller depend on state, a Lipschitz extension must be provided under [Appendix A.1](#sec-a-1); arbitrary cause classifiers must not be assumed to satisfy this automatically. During complete suspension, \(d^{\rm away}=0\).

**Interface capability limit.** If the adapter cannot determine any current cause scope, the default is \(c^{\rm away}=0\), hence \(D^{\rm att}=0\). In that case, an implementation cannot claim evidence-based additional disengagement from residual responses. This is an explicit conditional result; no data establish that unknown cases constitute “most” usage.

Even when \(D^{\rm att}=0\), source-attention competition \(h_j\to T_i\), natural recovery, and the long-term background remain active. Being busy may occupy \(R\) and reduce down-regulation while also decreasing attention to the original emotional source. The net effect cannot be inferred from \(c^{\rm away}=0\) alone as “negative emotion generally lasts longer.” An exponentially decaying cause ledger also cannot automatically be regarded as a true causal decomposition of mixed emotion.

<a id="sec-9-3"></a>

### 9.3 Mental Fatigue, Compensatory Effort, and Recovery Opportunity

Felt fatigue, regulatory intent, actual effort, and effort efficacy are represented separately. Load, compensation, and recovery functions are computed using the definitions below.

<a id="sec-9-3-1"></a>

#### 9.3.1 Inputs, Quantification, and Work Ownership

| Quantity | Domain, operational definition, and default rule |
|---|---|
| \(u_{\rm task},u_{\rm rec}\) | Nonnegative, with sum at most 1; effort fractions actually allocated to conversation / tasks / exploration and recall processing. The same work is counted once. Task difficulty, tokens, CPU use, message counts, and real elapsed time cannot substitute for these fractions |
| \(p_i^{\rm reg}\) | \([0,1]\), priority of the current down-regulation target; defaults to 1 on negative axes and 0 elsewhere. Not happiness or a memory learning rate |
| \(g_i^{\rm keep}\) | \([0,1]\), the degree to which the current goal calls for accepting / retaining the response; defaults to 0. Explicit retention requirements are encoded from evidence |
| \(\chi\) | \([0,1]\), willingness to compensate by increasing effort when regulation efficacy falls; defaults to .5, an intermediate engineering policy. 0 means no active compensation; 1 requests full compensation for reduced efficacy, still subject to the effort cap |
| \(q_{\rm rest}\) | \([0,1]\), the degree to which unallocated capacity is available for mental recovery; 0 means no recovery opportunity, and 1 means fully available. Without additional evidence, use the engineering default 1 and mark it as a default, not an observation. Known ongoing processing must still be recorded as actual effort; low \(q_{\rm rest}\) must not conceal unrecorded work |
| \(\theta_i^r\) | \([0,1]\), the deviation above background permitted by the down-regulation target; defaults to .1 on negative axes |
| Provenance labels for effort and goals | Direct activity record, the subject's established goal, inference, or engineering default. Retain evidence and the effective abstract-time interval. Linguistic estimates may use 0, .25, .5, .75, and 1 anchors, but these are not calibrated human measurement scales |

Current task success or failure is an independent factual input. Fatigue must not overwrite success rates, correctness of recalled content, or skills. A user reporting tiredness is not a direct observation of the subject's \(f\). Any future independent observation model for the subject's fatigue must separately define errors and state estimation; this specification does not reset \(f\) from a single statement.

<a id="sec-9-3-2"></a>

#### 9.3.2 Finite Effort and Compensable Regulation

Define
\[
R=1-u_{\rm task}-u_{\rm rec}\ge0,\qquad
r_i^{\rm reg}=p_i^{\rm reg}(1-g_i^{\rm keep})[e_i-m_i-\theta_i^r]_+,
\]
\[
S_r=\sum_{i\in\mathcal N}r_i^{\rm reg},\qquad
s_f=1+\rho_ff,\qquad C_f=s_f^\chi.
\]
Automatic regulation effort is
\[
\boxed{u_{{\rm reg},i}^{\rm auto}
=R u_{\max}^{\rm reg}
\frac{C_f r_i^{\rm reg}}{\delta_r+C_f S_r}},
\quad i\in\mathcal N,\quad \delta_r>0,\quad 0\le u_{\max}^{\rm reg}\le1.
\]
Other axes take zero. When \(S_r=0\), all entries are zero; division by \(S_r\) is unnecessary. A complete explicit effort vector may override this, including an intentional all-zero vector, provided \(u_{{\rm reg},i}\ge0,\sum_i u_{{\rm reg},i}\le R\). Complete suspension always sets it to zero. Restricting the default to negative axes is this module's choice of comfort objective, not a claim that humans down-regulate only negative emotions. This specification does not provide a complete theory of active up-regulation.

\[
u_\Sigma=u_{\rm task}+u_{\rm rec}+\sum_i u_{{\rm reg},i}\le1,
\qquad
\mathcal L=\omega_{\rm task}u_{\rm task}
+\omega_{\rm rec}u_{\rm rec}
+\omega_{\rm reg}\sum_i u_{{\rm reg},i}\in[0,1],
\]
\[
\boxed{\mathcal R_f=q_{\rm rest}(1-u_\Sigma).}
\]
\(u_\Sigma\) is total occupied effort; \(\mathcal L\) is the weighted load that accumulates fatigue. Reducing a category's \(\omega\) does not turn its still-working fraction into rest. A task that also redirects attention does not incur a second effort charge. Specific task effort still comes from the activity interface; these equations do not independently change external work scheduling or memory retrieval priorities.

\(\chi\) expresses compensatory intent for the current regulation target; it is not automatically inferred from joy \(J\) or interest \(I\). When \(\chi>0\), additional effort may be requested, but remains subject to total capacity. \(C_f\) follows the specified policy; motivation or reward is not converted directly into a reduction in fatigue.

\(g^{\rm keep}\) can reduce down-regulation. A valid current goal may change the default comfort objective.

<a id="sec-9-3-3"></a>

#### 9.3.3 Arbitrary Times and Recovery Conditions

Here \(t\ge s\) denote only two values of the abstract variable \(\tau\), unrelated to real-world time \(t_{\rm real}\). Along any admissible input/coupled solution, let
\[
A_f(\tau)=a_f\mathcal L(\tau),\qquad B_f(\tau)=b_f\mathcal R_f(\tau),
\quad H_f(t,s)=\int_s^t[A_f(u)+B_f(u)]\,du.
\]
Then
\[
\boxed{f(t)=e^{-H_f(t,s)}f(s)+
\int_s^t e^{-H_f(t,u)}A_f(u)\,du.}
\]
If the load varies with \(f\), this is an integral representation along the simultaneous solution. The load must not be assumed known in advance to bypass solving the coupling.

Fix actual load \(L\), total effort \(U\), and recovery quality \(q\), and let \(r=q(1-U)\) and \(k_f=a_fL+b_fr\). When \(k_f>0\):
\[
f^*=\frac{a_fL}{a_fL+b_fr},\qquad
f(\tau+h)=f^*+[f(\tau)-f^*]e^{-k_fh}.
\]
When \(k_f=0\), \(f\) remains constant; division by zero must not define an equilibrium. This closed-form formula is not an exact full-step solution for a general automatic-regulation feedback loop.

With no load,
\[
f(t)=f(s)\exp[-b_f\int_s^t\mathcal R_f(u)\,du].
\]
Existing fatigue is guaranteed to decay to zero only if cumulative recovery opportunity is sufficient. Absence of new events is not synonymous with complete recovery. With general nonzero load, one sufficient set of conditions is \(\mathcal L(\tau)\to0\) and, eventually, \(\mathcal R_f(\tau)\ge r_0>0\). Sustained full load without recovery yields \(f\to1\), not sudden incapacity in finite time.

\(a_f\) controls accumulation, \(b_f\) recovery, \(\rho_f\) differences in effort efficacy, and \(\chi\) compensation policy. These four roles are not interchangeable. When only the ten emotion axes are observed, fatigue level, effort, and efficacy may be confounded. Adding \(y_{\rm Fat}\) supplies a distinct observable, but does not automatically prove parameter identifiability.

<a id="sec-9-3-4"></a>

#### 9.3.4 Effort Provenance and Override Lifecycle

Emotion intensity, regulatory intent, actual regulation effort, and repetitive thinking are stored separately. Automatic-policy output is model-generated effort, not observed spontaneous human exertion.

The three computational cases have the following priority:

1. Complete suspension of active processing: all actual effort is zero under [9.1](#sec-9-1).
2. A valid, still-effective complete explicit regulation-effort vector is available: use it, including an all-zero vector, without adding automatic effort.
3. All other nonsuspended modes: use the automatic policy and current goals specified in [9.3.2](#sec-9-3-2). The default comfort objective remains effective, so absence of external effort values does not implicitly disable the fatigue–regulation pathway.

Each regulation-effort record requires **effort_source**:

| Value | Meaning |
|---|---|
| observed | The external activity interface has a verifiable effort-allocation record; this does not imply equivalence to calibrated human subjective effort |
| inferred | Estimated from activity evidence, retaining the basis, uncertainty, and effective abstract-time interval |
| policy_generated | Simulated regulation effort imposed by this model's automatic policy; an engineering-control result, not an observation |
| legacy_unspecified | For migration of existing states only: retains an old numerical value whose original effort provenance was not recorded; must not masquerade as an observation or evidence-based inference |

This label does not multiply \(\mathcal L\), \(K\), or the personality-learning rate. Different valid labels with the same valid numerical values produce the same trajectory. However, the enumeration domain and provenance eligibility remain part of the input contract:

- Accept only the four listed values. Reject other strings or missing values without implicitly mapping them to any category.
- policy_generated is produced by this module's automatic policy. observed and inferred must retain the corresponding activity evidence or inferential basis.
- legacy_unspecified arises only from migration of existing data under [16.4](#sec-16-4). New live inputs cannot use it to bypass provenance requirements.
- Rejection applies to the invalid input: its event or effort override is not executed. Continuous-time evolution under existing valid sources remains defined and is not implicitly frozen because of a data error.

A numerical implementation that ignores labels may compute the same trajectory for an invalid string, but this is not a conforming interface behavior. Engineering defaults for the goals \(p^{\rm reg},g^{\rm keep}\) must also retain their provenance. An absent supplied goal must not be rewritten as a participant's choice to regulate continuously.

**Override validity and selection after rejection.** Also retain the interface record
\[
O=(\widehat u_{\rm reg},\mathrm{source},\mathrm{evidence},
\tau_{\rm on},\tau_{\rm off})
\quad\text{or}\quad O=\varnothing,
\]
where \(\tau_{\rm off}>\tau_{\rm on}\), with interval
\([\tau_{\rm on},\tau_{\rm off})\). An explicitly unbounded endpoint \(+\infty\) is allowed; a missing endpoint must not automatically mean unbounded validity. For a new instance, \(O=\varnothing\). This is the complete representation of the effort override, not an additional psychological state.

- A new valid override is committed at its effective time and replaces the previous \(O\). For live input, the effective time is the commit time. An announced future input is committed only when it becomes effective, using order for simultaneous inputs. A replaced override does not resume automatically after its replacement expires.
- An invalid new input does not overwrite, revoke, or extend the existing \(O\). Continue using the previous \(O\) if it remains within its validity interval and satisfies current effort conservation.
- On valid revocation, expiry, or a valid activity update that makes the old effort exceed \(R\), the override becomes ineffective: set \(O=\varnothing\) and record the reason. Without an effective override, use the automatic policy. Do not silently scale the old effort vector, treat a new invalid value as zero, or retain an ineffective value indefinitely.
- Complete suspension still takes precedence over overrides. Suspension does not extend validity. Upon resumption, use only an override that remains effective and valid; otherwise use the automatic policy.

Let \(V_O(\tau)\in\{0,1\}\) denote an override that has been accepted, not replaced or revoked, remains within its validity interval, and satisfies the current effort domain. Final effort selection is
\[
u_{\rm reg}(\tau)=
\begin{cases}
0,&\text{active processing fully suspended},\\
\widehat u_{\rm reg}(\tau),&V_O(\tau)=1,\\
u_{\rm reg}^{\rm auto}(\tau),&\text{otherwise}.
\end{cases}
\]
This is an exclusive selection; the three branches are not added. If a new valid activity input conflicts with the old override, commit the activity update and cancellation of that override at the same time, without first advancing the state using excessive total effort. Rejection itself consumes no psychological time, creates no experience, and does not reset fatigue; time continues to evolve. Switches caused by modes, validity intervals, or invalidation following valid activity updates must satisfy the local-finiteness and timing conditions in [Appendix A.1](#sec-a-1). Any additional discrete guards determined by psychological state require separate unique switching semantics and existence-and-uniqueness conditions; the fixed-mode Lipschitz proof does not apply automatically.

\(u_{\rm task}=u_{\rm rec}=0\) means only that those two activity types are absent. If \(\sum u_{\rm reg}>0\), the subject is still performing simulated regulation work and cannot be described as doing no work or resting completely. Genuine zero effort is \(u_\Sigma=0\). When emotion remains high but no active regulation, recall, or other activity occurs, there is no pathway that accumulates fatigue directly from emotion intensity.

If external data establish actual processing in repetitive worry or recall, assign it to the existing \(u_{\rm task}\) or \(u_{\rm rec}\) according to its activity semantics. A nonzero anxiety axis alone must not generate such activity without evidence. The same episode of repetitive thinking must not be counted simultaneously as full recall effort and full regulation effort unless the activity interface explicitly allocates distinct work fractions. This preserves effort conservation, but does not claim a complete model of spontaneous rumination or natural human strategy selection.

Both the load term and recovery opportunity depend on effort; this does not add two copies of the work. The former increases fatigue, while the latter reduces current recovery opportunity. Nevertheless, using effort as a factor in both pathways is a specific engineering assumption whose relative strength requires human data. Absence of duplicate accounting does not establish psychological validity.

<a id="sec-9-4"></a>

### 9.4 Eligible Experience, Complete Profiles, and a Finite Current Summary

Each experience has an experience_id, source evidence_ids, a semantic phase, an abstract processing interval, reliability, relevance, used fractions, and a profile covering all axes. Eligibility requires a new actual interaction, task outcome, exploratory discovery, or reflection with a new verifiable conclusion. Actual repetitions of the same type may qualify; mere replay, reading, or paraphrase cannot create new fractions.

\[
c_l=q_l^E r_l^E\upsilon_l^E\in[0,1].
\]
The three terms are usable reliability, overall relevance to affective style, and the fraction not yet counted. Fractions across fragments of the same experience sum to at most one; each message or solver step does not receive a separate unit. The complete profile does not use per-axis \(M^E\) learning gates; external appraisal still uses \(M\). Ordinary or calm actual experience may also qualify. \(r_l^E\) must not be set to zero merely because emotion is weak, which would reintroduce selection bias toward intense emotion. Encode relevance from the experience domain and accepted goals, not the current intensity of an individual axis.

**Default sampling rule.** Register interactions, task attempts, exploration, or reflection with actual processing intervals as complete eligible semantic phases, using interval averages. Their individual messages or emotional peaks must not be recast as separate instantaneously completed experiences. A phase includes evidence-supported periods of low-intensity or calm participation and must not be cut short because emotion has declined. Passive waiting without new activity does not independently create a new experience allowance.

Use all ten axes of \(z_{li}=e_i^+\) only for an independent experience that genuinely completes instantaneously and has no definable processing interval; retain the reason for this exception. Missing processing intervals must not be set to zero for convenience, nor assigned an unsupported fixed duration. When interval data are missing, keep the experience pending and record the omission, or explicitly discard its unused fraction. Do not implicitly substitute the peak at completion.

For a continuous semantic phase \([a_l,b_l]\), \(b_l>a_l\), the activity interface supplies a participation mask \(a_l^{\rm exp}(\tau)\in\{0,1\}\). It is 1 during actual interaction, processing, permitted sustained attention, or exploration within the phase, and 0 during purely passive waiting or suspension. The mask follows activity records, not selection by valence, fatigue level, or whether an emotion threshold has been exceeded. Define
\[
D_l^{\rm exp}=\int_{a_l}^{b_l}a_l^{\rm exp}(\tau)d\tau,\qquad
I_{li}^{\rm exp}=\int_{a_l}^{b_l}a_l^{\rm exp}(\tau)e_i(\tau)d\tau.
\]
When \(D_l^{\rm exp}>0\), use
\[
\boxed{z_{li}=I_{li}^{\rm exp}/D_l^{\rm exp},\quad i\in\mathcal I.}
\]
If participation spans the entire interval, this reduces to the ordinary time average. When \(D_l^{\rm exp}=0\) or the activity interval is missing, do not evaluate the ratio. Use the instantaneous exception only if the experience genuinely meets its conditions; otherwise leave it pending or discard the unused fraction.
This records actual internal emotion, including low values and residual responses from other causes; it does not represent the event's causal contribution to each axis. Learning personality by specific cause requires a separately defined decomposition model. A complete profile does not replace causal decomposition.

By default, register once at semantic completion, using the capacity-replacement flow in [10.3](#sec-10-3). Do not retain a per-experience queue of future integration work. Archived records may contain the emotion module's own evidence references and fraction ledger, not raw memory data. Continuous registration requires an explicitly specified nonnegative rate \(j_l^E\) for that experience and the guarantee \(\int j_l^E\le c_l\). Its instantaneous profile \(z_{li}^{\rm stream}\in[0,1]\) defaults to the complete current \(e_i\); the same fraction must not later be registered again as a completion profile. Total rate and profile are
\[
j_E=\sum_lj_l^E,\qquad
z_i^E=\begin{cases}\sum_lj_l^Ez_{li}^{\rm stream}/j_E,&j_E>0,\\0,&j_E=0.\end{cases}
\]
If the average over the observed portion of an experience interval is used instead of the current value, retain the integral and interval; low values must still not be filtered out axis by axis. At zero rate, the profile has no effect.

Define the actual integration fraction as
\[
\boxed{u_E=g_E(u_{\rm task}+u_{\rm rec}),\quad g_E\in[0,1],\quad g_E^{\rm default}=1.}
\]
\(g_E\) is the fraction of existing work devoted to integrating affective experience, supplied as an externally encoded activity input. Emotion valence, regulation effort, or “accumulated failures” must not directly multiply an accelerator into it. A complete override must satisfy \(0\le u_E\le u_{\rm task}+u_{\rm rec}\) and include evidence of actual work. This is integration accompanying existing work, not another fatigue-producing effort allocation.

This equation contains no direct coupling to \(\sum u_{\rm reg}\). With identical task/recall effort, \(g_E,Q_E\), and parameters, positive and negative states have the same \(r_E\). This removes an engineering bias; it does not establish complete symmetry of positive and negative adaptation in humans. Overall trajectories may still differ because of emotional profiles, ongoing situations, or external activities.

Replay alone creates no new experience, but actual recall processing may integrate previously eligible summaries; this does not rewrite memory. During complete suspension, \(u_E=j_E=0\). Unintegrated summaries still fade passively according to \(\lambda_D\), while the learned \(b\) is retained.

Capacity replacement and time-based discarding only reduce unintegrated fractions. Changing an ID must not replenish them. They do not rewrite external memory content, sentiments, or historical evidence. Preserve this ownership distinction when integrating an external memory theory. The L1 summary is not a substitute for a human memory module.

<a id="sec-9-5"></a>

### 9.5 Partition Consistency and Limits of Experience Profiles

Partition the same complete phase into subintervals, with actual participation durations \(D_h^{\rm exp}\) and complete-axis averages \(z_i^{(h)}\). Then
\[
z_i=\frac{\sum_{h:D_h^{\rm exp}>0}D_h^{\rm exp}z_i^{(h)}}
{\sum_hD_h^{\rm exp}}.
\]
The denominator must be positive; a zero-participation segment must not be filled in as a zero-emotion sample.
Thus, a complete profile weighted by the correct durations and submitted once is unchanged by numerical integration partitioning. Each fragment must not receive its own experience allowance. While processing continues, retain only each phase's duration, ten-axis integrals, and fraction ledger; no additional theory of memory-content storage is introduced.

Registration once at completion and continuous registration are generally not equivalent, because discarding, integration, and capacity replacement differ during the interval. The choice must be explicit at experience onset and must not be changed midstream to submit duplicate claims. Time averaging reduces the weight of brief peaks; this specification does not replace the complete interval profile with a peak or endpoint.

The default submits the complete interval profile at completion; continuous registration may still be explicitly selected through the finite-fraction interface above. Actual semantic phases of different durations may receive equal total allowances. Consequently, this is not a personality theory that samples total lived time exactly. Calm experience is not generated automatically merely because no event occurs.

<a id="sec-10"></a>

## 10 Complete State and Continuous Equations

<a id="sec-10-1"></a>

### 10.1 State

The fixed parameters are \(b_0,g,\theta_{\rm reg},\ell\). Psychological states:
\[
X=(b,\{s_o\},m,e,w,f),\quad
b,m,e,w\in[0,1]^{10},\ s_o\in[0,1]^2,\ f\in[0,1],
\qquad x_i=\ell_i m_i+(1-\ell_i)w_i.
\]
L1 also retains fixed-dimensional sufficient statistics: amount awaiting integration \(Q_E\), emotion-weighted sums \(P_i^E\), cumulative integrated amount \(\Xi_E\), and cumulative discarded amount \(D_E\). The last two are accounting counters, not psychological time. Additional records cover sources, evidence deduplication, memory budgets, and summaries of unfinished experiences. These records add neither functional layers nor output axes; fatigue is output on the eleventh axis.

<a id="sec-10-2"></a>

### 10.2 Continuous Sources

Source \(j\) has \(M_{ji}\in\{0,1\}\), \(a_{ji}\in[0,1]\), \(h_j\ge0,\sum_jh_j\le1\), and \(0\le\bar v_j\le v_{\max}\). Define:
\[
v_j=h_j\bar v_j,\quad V_i=\sum_jv_jM_{ji},\quad
\alpha_i=V_i/v_{\max}\in[0,1],
\]
\[
A_i=\begin{cases}\sum_jv_jM_{ji}a_{ji}/V_i,&V_i>0,\\0,&V_i=0,\end{cases}
\]
\[
\boxed{T_i=(1-\alpha_i)m_i+
v_{\max}^{-1}\sum_jv_jM_{ji}a_{ji}.}
\]
Compute directly with the formula for \(T_i\) to avoid a zero denominator. Sufficient attention and maximum intensity permit \(\alpha=1,T=A\); there is no fixed 2/3 limit. Unevaluated axes are excluded; an evidence-supported zero target may be included. The spatial constraint still applies to \(x\).

<a id="sec-10-3"></a>

### 10.3 L1 Bounded Experience Integration

\[
0\le Q_E\le Q_{\max},\quad0\le P_i^E\le Q_E,\quad
\Xi_E,D_E\ge0,\quad Q_{\max}>0.
\]
\(\mu_E>0\) is the maximum experience amount integrable per unit of abstract time; \(\delta_E>0\) is the half-saturation experience amount; \(\lambda_D>0\) is the discard rate for unintegrated summaries; \(u_E\in[0,1]\) is the actual integration-activity fraction. Let
\[
a_E^c=\frac{\mu_Eu_E}{\delta_E+Q_E},\qquad
r_E=a_E^c Q_E\le\mu_E,\qquad H_b>0.
\]
\(a_E^c\) is an experience-consumption rate coefficient, not appraisal or external event intensity.

An eligible continuous experience-registration rate \(j_E(\tau)\ge0\) and complete-axis profile \(z_i^E(\tau)\in[0,1]\) may be supplied externally, subject to the total-fraction-per-experience contract in [9.4](#sec-9-4). The default is \(j_E=0\), with jump registration at semantic completion. Continuous equations:
\[
k_E=a_E^c+\lambda_D+j_E/Q_{\max},
\]
\[
\boxed{\dot Q_E=j_E-k_EQ_E,\qquad
\dot P_i^E=j_Ez_i^E-k_EP_i^E,}
\]
\[
\boxed{\dot\Xi_E=r_E,\qquad
\dot D_E=\lambda_DQ_E+j_EQ_E/Q_{\max},}
\]
\[
\boxed{\dot b_i=\frac{a_E^c}{H_b}
\left[(1-\rho_b)b_{0,i}Q_E+\rho_bP_i^E-Q_Eb_i\right],\quad0\le\rho_b<1.}
\]
The final equation is directly zero at \(Q_E=0\), without division by zero. For \(Q_E>0\), it is equivalent to
\[
\dot b_i=(r_E/H_b)(t_i^E-b_i),\quad
t_i^E=(1-\rho_b)b_{0,i}+\rho_bP_i^E/Q_E.
\]
\[
\mathcal B_i=[(1-\rho_b)b_{0,i},(1-\rho_b)b_{0,i}+\rho_b].
\]

Generate the fraction \(c_l\in[0,1]\) and complete-axis profile \(z_{li}\in[0,1]\) of a new eligible experience under [9.4](#sec-9-4). Let \(q_l=\exp(-c_l/Q_{\max})\). The registration map is:
\[
\boxed{Q_E^+=q_lQ_E^-+Q_{\max}(1-q_l),}
\]
\[
\boxed{P_i^{E,+}=q_lP_i^{E,-}+Q_{\max}(1-q_l)z_{li},}
\]
\[
D_E^+=D_E^-+c_l-(Q_E^+-Q_E^-),\qquad
b^+=b^-,\quad\Xi_E^+=\Xi_E^-.
\]
It is the exact flow, parameterized by experience amount \(s\in[0,c_l]\), of
\(dQ_E/ds=1-Q_E/Q_{\max}\) and
\(dP_i^E/ds=z_{li}-P_i^E/Q_{\max}\).
Capacity replaces unintegrated summaries without immediately rewriting the already formed \(b\) or deleting external memories. The discarded amount is nonnegative, and exact accounting conservation holds:
\[
Q_E(\tau)+[\Xi_E(\tau)-\Xi_E(0)]+[D_E(\tau)-D_E(0)]
=Q_E(0)+\sum_{\tau_l\le\tau}c_l+\int_0^\tau j_E(u)\,du.
\]

The complete-axis profile has no emotion-type mask and includes axes actually weak or absent at that time. This distinguishes an internally known low value from an unknown external appraisal; the latter still must not be filled with zero. Frequency means the proportions of different states among eligible experience samples, not conversation turns used as time. Sampling bias and residual emotion also enter the profile, so it cannot claim to uniquely recover objective environmental event frequencies.

This layer requires only \(1+10\) current summary values, without a per-experience integration queue. The emotion module's own traceability records may be archived separately; original external memories and evidence must not thereby be moved, deleted, or rewritten. Capacity, exponential replacement, and the discard rate for unintegrated experience are engineering choices, not established laws of human memory capacity or personality forgetting.

<a id="sec-10-4"></a>

### 10.4 L2 sentiments

Direct object evidence \(q^{\rm learn}_{jo}\in[0,1]\), pre-bias learning targets \(z^\pm_{jo}\in[0,1]\), and object weights follow [Section 6](#sec-6):
\[
W_o=\sum_jv_j\omega_{jo}q^{\rm learn}_{jo},\quad
\beta_{jo}=\frac{v_j\omega_{jo}q^{\rm learn}_{jo}}{1+W_o},
\]
\[
\boxed{\dot s_o^\pm=\lambda_s^\pm(s_{o,0}^\pm-s_o^\pm)
+\nu_s^\pm\sum_j\beta_{jo}(z^\pm_{jo}-s_o^\pm).}
\]
For replay alone or mere retention of an existing concern, use \(q^{\rm learn}=0\). Continuous learning requires genuinely continuous direct evidence and must not duplicate the same instantaneous evidence. The default is \(\lambda_s^\pm=0\), retaining the state without new evidence.

<a id="sec-10-5"></a>

### 10.5 L3 Long-Term Background

\[
\boxed{\dot m_i=\lambda_{m,i}[(1-\kappa)b_i+\kappa e_i-m_i],\quad0\le\kappa<1.}
\]
At events, \(m^+=m^-\). The background integrates temporal history; events, tasks, concerns, and recall may all affect it through \(e\).

<a id="sec-10-6"></a>

### 10.6 L4 Proximal Response and Down-Regulation

\(\mathcal N=\{F,A,S,D,H,G\}\). Deliberate regulation effort \(u_{{\rm reg},i}\) is generated under [9.3](#sec-9-3):
\[
K_i=\frac{\eta_i u_{{\rm reg},i}}{1+\rho_ff},\qquad
\eta_i\ge0,\ \rho_f\ge0.
\]
By default, only \(\mathcal N\) have deliberate down-regulation targets, with \(\eta_i=0\) on other axes. This is a default preference, not a claim that humans cannot regulate positive emotion.

Also, [9.2](#sec-9-2) defines the evidence-based attentional-disengagement fraction \(d_i^{\rm away}\in[0,1]\). Let
\[
