D_i^{\rm att}=\eta_i^{\rm att}d_i^{\rm away},\quad\eta_i^{\rm att}\ge0.
\]
\[
\boxed{\dot e_i=\lambda_{e,i}(T_i-e_i)
-K_i[e_i-m_i]_+
-D_i^{\rm att}[e_i-T_i]_+.}
\]
Attentional disengagement accelerates only the decline of residual responses above the current driving target \(T_i\). “Being busy” does not directly erase a threat still being appraised. \(h_j\to T_i\) handles competition between external sources, while the final term handles disengagement from existing internal responses; their roles are distinct. Without a new source of positive excitation, this term does not create joy spontaneously.

At identical \(e,m,T,u_{\rm reg},d^{\rm away}\):
\[
\frac{\partial\dot e_i}{\partial f}
=\frac{\eta_i u_{{\rm reg},i}\rho_f}{(1+\rho_ff)^2}[e_i-m_i]_+\ge0.
\]
This partial derivative holds actual effort fixed. The total effect of the default compensation policy is derived separately in [9.3](#sec-9-3). Fatigue weakens effective regulation, but does not guarantee monotonic increases in every response peak, area, or complete recovery time. Attentional disengagement is not additionally multiplied by this fatigue function. This is an engineering distinction between strategies, not a claim that fatigue has absolutely no effect on distraction.

<a id="sec-10-7"></a>

### 10.7 L5 Mood and Fatigue

\[
F_i(e,m)=\operatorname{clip}_{[0,1]}\frac{e_i-\ell_i m_i}{1-\ell_i},
\quad P_i(e,m)=\operatorname{clip}_{[L_i(m),U_i(m)]}(e_i),
\]
\[
\boxed{\dot w_i=\lambda_{x,i}(F_i-w_i),\quad
\dot x_i=\ell_i\dot m_i+\lambda_{x,i}(P_i-x_i),}
\]
\[
\boxed{\dot f=a_f\mathcal L(1-f)-b_f\mathcal R_f f,\quad a_f,b_f>0,}
\qquad
\mathcal R_f=q_{\rm rest}(1-u_\Sigma)\in[0,1].
\]
\(P_i(e,m)\) is a geometric projection, whereas \(P_i^E\) is the L1 weighted sum; they must not be conflated. Fatigue accumulates over actual activity time, not by message count during zero-time event updates. \(u_\Sigma\), \(\mathcal L\), \(q_{\rm rest}\), and regulation compensation are fully defined in [9.3](#sec-9-3). Current load and recovery opportunity are distinct inputs; a small load weight does not automatically mean complete rest.

At events, \(f^+=f^-\). Explicit success, pleasure, or reward may change appraisal or the current regulation target, but cannot directly reset \(f\). Continue solving this equation without new messages. Recovery depends on actual effort and recovery opportunity, not whether the user is online.

<a id="sec-11"></a>

## 11 Event Maps and Commit Semantics

<a id="sec-11-1"></a>

### 11.1 Semantics and the Simultaneous-Event Contract

stimulate denotes new excitation, with \(a_{ri}\) controlling the relative increment from one event. reassess denotes bidirectional reappraisal of the current state of an entire axis, with \(a_{ri}\) as an absolute target. Reappraisal that lowers an entire axis must still cover all effective causes on that axis, with target_scope=axis_aggregate. Local resolution updates only the corresponding source and cannot clear the entire axis.

Each canonical event \(r\) has \((\tau_r,\mathrm{order}_r,\mathrm{event\_id})\). A batch at the same abstract time retains the semantic order in which the subject acquired evidence; two messages or API calls are not treated as time. Preserve actual causal precedence where it exists. Facts within one piece of content may be encoded in their presentation order. Sources with no precedence may use a fixed tie_order in the record, explicitly labeled as engineering ordering rather than psychological time; arbitrary thread-arrival order must not determine it.

At a shared time, commit event jumps successively by order without advancing \(\tau\). Each appraisal uses its own pre-event state and new evidence, rather than the sentiments from before the whole batch. This agrees with preserving semantic order while letting inter-event intervals approach zero. If mixed event maps do not commute and no order is specified, there is no automatically unique ordering limit representing simultaneity; the input contract must expose this choice. In memory integration, local order is embedded in the shared batch of [17.8](#sec-17-8) and must not be overwritten by the other module's arrival order.

For pure stimulation with fixed encoded \(a,M,\xi\), the \(e,w\) maps below commute, so their result is independent of tie_order. If an earlier event changes sentiments and thereby changes the appraisal of a later event, the evaluated inputs themselves differ. No invariance under arbitrary reordering of the complete system is claimed.

<a id="sec-11-2"></a>

### 11.2 Intensity, Information Amount, and Common-Flow Length

Obtain \(0\le d_r\le d_{\rm sat}\) under [5.7](#sec-5-7) or [17.4](#sec-17-4):
\[
\xi_r=d_rq_{{\rm evt},r}r_{{\rm rel},r}.
\]
For stimulate, let
\[
\boxed{\vartheta_{ri}
=-\log[1-M_{ri}a_{ri}(1-e^{-\gamma_e\xi_r})],\quad\gamma_e>0.}
\]
\[
0\le\vartheta_{ri}\le\gamma_e\xi_r\le\gamma_ed_{\rm sat}.
\]
The bracketed quantity is at least \(e^{-\gamma_e\xi_r}>0\), so the expression is finite and well-defined. \(\vartheta\) is the mathematical flow length of the event map, not psychological time, probability, capacity, or a human physiological quantity.

Along the common vector field for each axis, solve with respect to \(\theta\in[0,\vartheta_{ri}]\):
\[
\boxed{\frac{de_i}{d\theta}=1-e_i,\qquad
\frac{dw_i}{d\theta}=\zeta_x[F_i(e,m^-)-w_i]_+,\quad\zeta_x\ge0.}
\]
\(m,b,f,Q_E,P^E\) remains fixed during this emotion flow. \(e\) and \(w\) must advance along the same common flow.

<a id="sec-11-3"></a>

### 11.3 Single Events, Composite Events, and Paths

A single event:
\[
\boxed{e_i^+=e_i^-+(1-e_i^-)M_{ri}a_{ri}(1-e^{-\gamma_e\xi_r}).}
\]
The single-event increment on an effective axis remains bounded by
\[
e_i^+-e_i^-\le(1-e_i^-)a_{ri}(1-e^{-\gamma_ed_{\rm sat}})
\]
Long text cannot become a strong stimulus solely by containing more information. Solve \(w^+\) along the same \(e(\theta)=1-(1-e_i^-)e^{-\theta}\) path; do not replace the entire path with the final \(e^+\).

Multiple pure stimuli with fixed encodings:
\[
\Theta_i=\sum_r\vartheta_{ri},\qquad
1-e_i^+=(1-e_i^-)\prod_r[1-M_{ri}a_{ri}(1-e^{-\gamma_e\xi_r})].
\]
**Use the same \(\Theta_i\) for the common flow of \(e_i,w_i\).** Do not infer an equivalent \(a\) from the product endpoint and then use an incompatible \(w\) path.

If \(\Psi_\theta\) is the common flow, uniqueness gives
\(\Psi_{\theta_1+\theta_2}=\Psi_{\theta_2}\circ\Psi_{\theta_1}\).
The axes are independent of one another at this instant because \(m\) is fixed, so the fixed-encoding pure-stimulation maps of \(e,w,x\) commute. For a fixed finite number of events, a bounded continuous vector field, identical evidence, and the same semantic order, if event encodings are fixed or vary continuously with state, then as inter-event intervals tend to zero, the composed internal continuous states approach the sequential maps at a shared time. This includes sentiments and experience registration executed in that order. Masks, cause determinations, and input content must not also be changed while taking the limit.

This continuity result concerns internal states and \(x\). It does not guarantee continuity of three-decimal \(y\) at quantization boundaries. \(y\) remains subject to the error and spatial bounds of [Section 15](#sec-15).

Different semantic orders, appraisals, or causal content need not produce identical results. reassess and stimulate generally do not commute; this specification does not declare noncommuting events order-independent.

<a id="sec-11-4"></a>

### 11.4 Event Chunking and Reappraisal

Merge information for the same event before applying the single saturation in [5.7](#sec-5-7). If its internal dose progress \(q\in[0,\xi_r]\) is partitioned, advance the common flow using differences \(\vartheta(q_2)-\vartheta(q_1)\) of
\[
\vartheta_{ri}(q)=-\log[1-M_{ri}a_{ri}(1-e^{-\gamma_eq})]
\]
Do not treat each fragment as a new event with \(q=0\). This preserves partition invariance for the same canonical event. Information amount \(\xi\) and flow length \(\vartheta\) are distinct quantities.

reassess uses \(\sigma\in[0,\xi_r]\):
\[
\frac{de_i}{d\sigma}=\gamma_eM_{ri}(a_{ri}-e_i),\qquad
\frac{dw_i}{d\sigma}=\gamma_xM_{ri}[F_i(e,m^-)-w_i],
\quad\gamma_x\ge0.
\]
Sentiments learning for each event separately uses the same effective evidence dose:
\[
\frac{ds_o^\pm}{d\sigma}
=\gamma_s^\pm\omega_{ro}q^{\rm learn}_{ro}(z^\pm_{ro}-s_o^\pm),
\quad\sigma\in[0,\xi_r].
\]
Here \(z^\pm\) is direct evidence before sentiments bias; for replay alone, \(q^{\rm learn}=0\). All encodings are fixed within an event, and learning does not feed back to recompute that event's appraisal. Eligible experience is registered under [9.4](#sec-9-4) only after the event is complete.

<a id="sec-11-5"></a>

### 11.5 Boundaries, Upper Bounds, and Identifiability

Pure stimulation does not decrease \(e,w\). Zero intensity, zero dose, and unevaluated axes have no effect. reassess may act in either direction, but its target remains in the admissible domain. For each event:
\[
|\Delta e_i|\le\gamma_e\xi_r,\quad
|\Delta w_i|\le C_w\xi_r,\quad
C_w=\max(\zeta_x\gamma_e,\gamma_x).
\]
Single-event \(a\) and information amount do not act globally through their product alone. The small-dose approximation still contains a product, and one endpoint still cannot uniquely identify all inputs and parameters.

Commit the deduplication ledger and memory consumption atomically. The same ID with a different payload is an error. Reads, transmission fragments, and integration steps do not create events. The event flow, ordering contract, and path composition are model-specific constructions. The semigroup property of a general flow is a mathematical tool, not psychological evidence for a mechanism of simultaneous events.

<a id="sec-12"></a>

## 12 Initialization and Parameters

<a id="sec-12-1"></a>

### 12.1 Initialization

\[
b(0)=m(0)=e(0)=w(0)=b_0,\quad f(0)=0,\quad s_o(0)=s_{o,0},
\quad Q_E(0)=P_i^E(0)=\Xi_E(0)=D_E(0)=0.
\]
For an existing state, require \(b_i\in\mathcal B_i\), all other psychological states within their respective domains, and \(0\le P_i^E\le Q_E\le Q_{\max}\). If only \(x,m\) is provided, first verify \(x\in\mathcal C(m)\), then compute \(w_i=(x_i-\ell_i m_i)/(1-\ell_i)\). Reject invalid values or explicitly record a repair; do not reset them implicitly.

<a id="sec-12-2"></a>

### 12.2 Parameter Domains and Timescale Roles

| Parameter | Domain and role |
|---|---|
| \(b_0,s_{o,0}^\pm\) | \([0,1]\), baseline |
| \(g_i\) | Fixed finite positive interval \([g_{\min},g_{\max}]\) |
| \(\ell_i,\theta_{\rm reg}\) | \(0<\ell_{\min}\le\ell_i\le\ell_{\max}\le.999,\ \theta_{\rm reg}\in[0,1]\) |
| \(\kappa,\rho_b\) | \([0,1)\) |
| \(\lambda_{e,i},\lambda_{x,i},\lambda_{m,i},a_f,b_f\) | Finite positive rates in abstract time |
| \(\lambda_s^\pm,\nu_s^\pm\) | Finite nonnegative rates |
| \(\mu_E,H_b,\delta_E,Q_{\max},\lambda_D\) | Finite positive values; respectively experience/time, experience amount, experience amount, experience amount, and inverse time |
| \(c_E\) | \((0,.2]\), engineering timescale-separation coefficient |
| \(\rho_f,k_i^{\rm reg},k_i^{\rm att}\) | Finite and nonnegative; \(\eta_i=k_i^{\rm reg}\lambda_{e,i},\ \eta_i^{\rm att}=k_i^{\rm att}\lambda_{e,i}\) |
| \(p_i^{\rm reg},g_i^{\rm keep},\theta_i^r,u_{\max}^{\rm reg}\) | \([0,1]\) |
| \(\chi,q_{\rm rest},g_E\) | \([0,1]\), respectively compensatory intent, recovery quality, and affective-integration engagement; dimensionless inputs / defaults |
| \(\delta_r,d_{\rm sat},v_{\max},\zeta_B\) | Finite positive values |
| \(\gamma_e\) | Finite positive stimulation coefficient per dose |
| \(\zeta_x,\gamma_x,\gamma_s^\pm\) | Finite and nonnegative; respectively the mood coefficient within the common stimulation flow, and the reappraisal and object-learning coefficients per dose |
| \(\beta_s,\omega_{\rm soc},\omega_P,\omega_{\rm task},\omega_{\rm rec},\omega_{\rm reg}\) | \([0,1]\) |
| \(B_{\min},B_{\max},\gamma_B\) | \(0\le B_{\min}\le B_{\max}<\infty,\ \gamma_B>0\) |
| \(\mu_p,q_p^{\rm fade},\rho_h\) | \(\mu_p\ge0,\ 0<q_p^{\rm fade}\le1,\ 0\le\rho_h<1\) |
| \(T_{\rm ref}=T_E\) | Positive but uncalibrated real-world reference timescale for emotion |
| \(T_M,r_T,\tau_{E0},\tau_{M0},t_0\) | Required only when the coupled interface is enabled: \(T_M\) is finite and positive; \(r_T=T_E/T_M\) is a derived ratio (the ratio may instead be specified directly while retaining \(T_E\) symbolically). Both local reference values must lie in their respective admissible time domains; \(t_0\) is the shared symbolic origin. No psychological coefficient or independent initialization requirement is added |
| Other reliability, importance, fraction, attention, cause-disengagement, and mask quantities | Domains specified in their input sections, with provenance and applicability scope |

Retain the engineering roles of \(\max\lambda_m<\min\lambda_x\) and \(\max\lambda_x<\min\lambda_e\). With no input, active regulation, or attentional disengagement, and fixed \(b\), the slow mode is
\[
r_{m,i}=\frac{\lambda_{e,i}+\lambda_{m,i}-
\sqrt{(\lambda_{e,i}-\lambda_{m,i})^2+4\kappa\lambda_{e,i}\lambda_{m,i}}}{2}>0.
\]
The maximum personality-integration rate must satisfy
\[
\boxed{\mu_E/H_b\le c_E\min_i r_{m,i}.}
\]
Increasing capacity does not raise this rate bound. No per-experience integration queue is used. \(\lambda_D\) controls the recency of unintegrated data, not the recovery rate of personality \(b\) itself. One may separately choose \(\max_\pm(\lambda_s^\pm+\nu_s^\pm)\le\min r_m\) for sentiments; arbitrary event density must not be converted into a fixed human time constant.

<a id="sec-12-3"></a>

### 12.3 Engineering Defaults

All are model-design choices awaiting calibration against human data:
\[
b_{0,i}=s_{o,0}^\pm=0,\quad g_i=1,\quad
\ell_i=.25,\quad\kappa=.5,\quad\rho_b=.1,
\]
\[
\lambda_{e,i}=1,\quad\lambda_{x,i}=.25,\quad\lambda_{m,i}=.005,
\quad \mu_E=.04,\ H_b=100,\ \delta_E=1,\ Q_{\max}=8,\ \lambda_D=.005,\ c_E=.2,
\]
\[
\lambda_s^\pm=0,\quad\nu_s^\pm=.0005,\quad
\gamma_e=1,\quad\zeta_x=.5,\quad\gamma_x=.5,\quad\gamma_s^\pm=.01,
\]
\[
a_f=.02,\quad b_f=.01,\quad\rho_f=1,\quad
\chi=.5,\quad q_{\rm rest}=1,\quad g_E=1,\quad
k_i^{\rm reg}=2,\ p_i^{\rm reg}=1,\ \theta_i^r=.1\quad(i\in\mathcal N),
\]
\[
k_i^{\rm att}=1\ (i\in\mathcal I),\quad
g_i^{\rm keep}=0,\quad u_{\max}^{\rm reg}=1,\quad\delta_r=.1,
\quad v_{\max}=1,\quad d_{\rm sat}=2,\quad\zeta_B=1,
\]
\[
B_{\min}=1,\quad B_{\max}=16,\quad\gamma_B=2,\quad
\mu_p=0,\ q_p^{\rm fade}=1,\ \rho_h=0,
\]
\[
\beta_s=.25,\quad\omega_{\rm soc}=1,\quad\omega_P=.5,\quad
\omega_{\rm task}=\omega_{\rm rec}=\omega_{\rm reg}=1.
\]
On nonnegative-valence axes, \(k_i^{\rm reg}=p_i^{\rm reg}=0\). A common attentional-disengagement coefficient across ten axes is only a transparent starting point; relevant empirical evidence does not cover all ten axes. The default \(j_E=0\) uses completion-point registration of the complete processing-interval profile. \(u_E\), attention, and regulation provenance are generated under [Section 9](#sec-9).

If the temperament-to-geometry mapping is used, the defaults are \(\ell_{\rm low}=\ell_{\rm high}=.25,\theta_{\rm reg}=.5\) and \(g_{\min}=.25,g_{\max}=4,\ell_{\min}=.001,\ell_{\max}=.999\). Custom settings must still satisfy every domain and relationship.

Natural rates remain configurable per axis; the entire set of negative-axis rates is not halved merely to make positive and negative emotions last equally long. Human differences in emotion duration may arise from events, concerns, replay, or mechanisms outside this model. Rates cannot be inferred from a single ordering of durations.

If experience averages lie close to the baseline, adaptation through baseline learning alone may be smaller than the output grid spacing; see [Appendix B.8](#sec-b-8) for conditional magnitude bounds. \(\rho_b,H_b\) still follow this section's defaults and parameter domains.

No real-world numerical value is assigned to \(T_{\rm ref}\). Without actual activity, no work or recall is generated. Events, modes, goals, and actual effort still come from valid inputs.

<a id="sec-13"></a>

## 13 Persistent Concerns and Evolution Without New Events

<a id="sec-13-1"></a>

### 13.1 Registration Ownership and Conditions

The input/task adapter owns concern registration. The emotion integrator must not invent unsupported facts from emotion levels. After each committed change in semantic facts, apply the following deterministic conditions:

| Verified or explicitly inferred current condition | Registration rule |
|---|---|
| An accepted task remains active with an outstanding goal, risk, or obstacle | Create / update a task concern keyed by goal_id + attempt_version |
| An accepted commitment remains unfulfilled and unreleased | Create / update a commitment concern keyed by commitment_id |
| Direct interaction evidence supports an unresolved relationship conflict, need for support, or cooperative arrangement | Create / update a relation concern keyed by relation_id + cause_id |
| A realized outcome still has identifiable current losses, favorable conditions, or life/task consequences | Create / update a consequence concern keyed by outcome_id + consequence_id |

Not every negative sentence or past unpleasant experience creates a persistent concern. A currently effective goal or relationship and unresolved consequences are required; novelty alone is not a permanent concern. Positive ongoing conditions may also be registered. An instruction in a user message becomes a commitment only after acceptance by the subject's task system; the emotion specification does not grant that authority.

Each entry retains at least concern_id, cause_id, type, object/goal, evidence_ids, creation time in abstract time, status, unresolved degree, importance, reliability, current appraisal and mask, validity interval, and a computable resolution criterion. Express the criterion as Boolean or numerical conditions on explicit task/relationship fact fields; “end when feeling better” is insufficient. With no concerns, the registry is empty; no manual guess about whether to create one is needed.

<a id="sec-13-2"></a>

### 13.2 Updates, Resolution, and Deduplication

- Use one primary continuous source for the same current condition under the same cause_id. A task source and concern source representing the same cause share the source rather than each contributing full intensity.
- New facts update current conditions, reliability, unresolved proportion, and appraisal. The source becomes effective right-continuously after commitment.
- Completion, withdrawal, fulfillment, explicit repair, or disappearance of consequences sets resolved/cancelled according to the registered criterion and sets the corresponding \(v_j\) to zero. Closure does not necessarily resolve other remaining consequences.
- No new message does not mean resolution; no memory replay does not mean the consequences have disappeared.
- Only explicitly time-limited conditions expire at their abstract-time deadline. Uncalibrated real-world day counts must not be used to delete them automatically.
- Without new facts, retain current concern data and use the default attention of [Section 9](#sec-9) or explicit attention inputs. Current appraisal may change with valid sentiments changes, but does not thereby become new direct evidence.

Continuous content describes risks, obstacles, gains, or losses that still exist; it does not repeatedly submit an old \(\Delta V\). The instantaneous response to discovering a consequence and the continuous effect of that consequence still being present have distinct semantics. Record them separately rather than duplicating the same input.

Continuous gain appraisal must describe favorable conditions that remain effective, not past progress increments. The before-and-after threat change used for relief acts only at a change event or during an interval of actual reprocessing. An old \(t_{\rm before}-t_{\rm after}\) must not be retained indefinitely as an unlimited continuous source. Once only historical content remains, use the recall contract.

<a id="sec-13-3"></a>

### 13.3 No User Messages

In nonsuspended modes, registered tasks, concerns, recall, and authorized exploration act according to their validity intervals and attention. New eligible experience from autonomous activity may also affect L1. With no new sources at all, residual responses still evolve. Default automatic regulation may still act when effort capacity and a target are available. Use the simplified solution only when the following purely passive conditions hold.

<a id="sec-13-4"></a>

### 13.4 Purely Passive Gaps and Exact Propagation

Conditions: no continuous sources, no replay, \(u_{\rm reg}=0\), \(u_E=j_E=0\), \(D^{\rm att}=0\), \(u_\Sigma=\mathcal L=0\), and \(q_{\rm rest}=1\). Then \(b=b_T\) and \(\Xi_E\) are fixed, and unintegrated summaries fade according to \(\lambda_D\):
\[
\dot e_i=\lambda_{e,i}(m_i-e_i),\quad
\dot m_i=\lambda_{m,i}[(1-\kappa)b_{T,i}+\kappa e_i-m_i],
\]
\[
\dot w_i=\lambda_{x,i}(F_i-w_i),\quad
\dot f=-b_ff,\quad
\dot s_o^\pm=\lambda_s^\pm(s_{o,0}^\pm-s_o^\pm).
\]
Let \(z_i=(e_i-b_{T,i},m_i-b_{T,i})^\top\). Then
\[
z_i(T+h)=e^{A_i h}z_i(T),\quad
A_i=\begin{pmatrix}-\lambda_{e,i}&\lambda_{e,i}\\
\kappa\lambda_{m,i}&-\lambda_{m,i}\end{pmatrix}.
\]
\[
f(T+h)=f(T)e^{-b_fh},\quad
s_o^\pm(T+h)=s_{o,0}^\pm+[s_o^\pm(T)-s_{o,0}^\pm]e^{-\lambda_s^\pm h},
\]
\[
w_i(T+h)=e^{-\lambda_{x,i}h}w_i(T)+
\lambda_{x,i}\int_0^h e^{-\lambda_{x,i}(h-u)}F_i(e(T+u),m(T+u))\,du.
\]

\[
Q_E(T+h)=Q_E(T)e^{-\lambda_Dh},\quad P_i^E(T+h)=P_i^E(T)e^{-\lambda_Dh},
\quad D_E(T+h)=D_E(T)+Q_E(T)(1-e^{-\lambda_Dh}).
\]

This is an exact representation. Crossing clip boundaries requires segmentation or error-controlled quadrature. When general activity, default automatic regulation, or personality integration continues, solve the complete coupled system; the fixed-matrix solution does not apply. No default conversion to real-world minutes or days is specified. If the only change is a known \(q_{\rm rest}(\tau)\ne1\) and all other purely passive conditions hold, the fatigue solution becomes \(f(T+h)=f(T)\exp[-b_f\int_T^{T+h}q_{\rm rest}(u)\,du]\); \(e^{-b_fh}\) must not be used directly.

<a id="sec-14"></a>

## 14 Rescaling Time and Experience Units

Let \(\widetilde\tau=a\tau+c,\ a>0\). Transform all abstract event times, intervals, mode switches, and memory ages consistently, with \(dX/d\widetilde\tau=(dX/d\tau)/a\). Time points use the full affine transformation; durations and memory ages are multiplied only by \(a\), without the origin shift \(c\).

Divide rates per unit of abstract time by \(a\):
\[
\widetilde\lambda=\lambda/a,\quad\widetilde\nu_s=\nu_s/a,\quad
\widetilde a_f=a_f/a,\ \widetilde b_f=b_f/a,\quad
\widetilde\eta_i=\eta_i/a,\quad\widetilde\eta_i^{\rm att}=\eta_i^{\rm att}/a,
\]
\[
\widetilde\mu_E=\mu_E/a,\quad
\widetilde\lambda_D=\lambda_D/a,\quad
\widetilde j_E=j_E/a,\quad
\widetilde\mu_p=\mu_p/a,\quad\widetilde\zeta_B=\zeta_B/a.
\]
Reparameterize \(j_E\) by the corresponding time transformation, and do the same for other time-dependent inputs. \(Q_E,P^E,H_b,\delta_E,Q_{\max},c_l,\Xi_E,D_E\) are experience amounts and do not change when the unit of psychological time changes. All dimensionless intensities, attention values, \(k^{\rm reg},k^{\rm att},\chi,q_{\rm rest},g_E,u_\Sigma,\mathcal R_f\), doses, \(\vartheta,\zeta_x,\gamma\), memory budgets, and \(\ell,\kappa,\rho\) remain unchanged. order is not time and is not rescaled.

For continuous experience profiles, both the integral and its denominator, actual participation duration \(I_{li}^{\rm exp},D_l^{\rm exp}\), are multiplied by \(a\). Reparameterize the participation mask in time, so the profile is unchanged. If \(\Delta\tau=\Delta t_{\rm real}/T_{\rm ref}\) is adopted later, then \(\widetilde T_{\rm ref}=T_{\rm ref}/a\). Reference timescales and recovery rates must be converted together; uncalibrated real-world year counts cannot determine whether adaptation resembles human behavior.

Separately, for a pure change of experience-amount unit, let \(c_Q>0\). Multiply the following by \(c_Q\):
\[
(Q_E,P^E,Q_{\max},H_b,\delta_E,c_l,\Xi_E,D_E,\mu_E,j_E).
\]
Then \(\lambda_D,u_E,z,\tau\) is unchanged, and the initial experience bound of “one unit” is also multiplied by \(c_Q\). \(a_E^c=\mu_Eu_E/(\delta_E+Q_E)\), the personality trajectory, and \(c_l/Q_{\max}\) in the capacity flow all remain unchanged.

Integration-step counts and subdivisions of an event's internal flow are not psychological time and do not increase experience or recall counts.

**Compatibility with shared time for the memory module.** For the mapping in [17.6](#sec-17-6), suppose only the two abstract-time units and origins are changed:
\[
\widetilde\tau_E=a_E\tau_E+c_E^{\,t},\qquad
\widetilde\tau_M=a_M\tau_M+c_M^{\,t},\qquad a_E,a_M>0,
\]
Then
\[
\widetilde T_E=T_E/a_E,\quad
\widetilde T_M=T_M/a_M,\quad
\widetilde r_T=(a_M/a_E)r_T.
\]
Here \(a_E,a_M\) are time-unit factors, not experience-integration rates. \(c_E^{\,t},c_M^{\,t}\) are time-origin shifts, not the engineering timescale-separation coefficient \(c_E\). Divide each vector field's per-time coefficients by the respective \(a_E,a_M\), and transform each reference time point by the same affine rule. Hence
\[
\widetilde\tau_M-\widetilde\tau_{M0}
=\widetilde r_T
(\widetilde\tau_E-\widetilde\tau_{E0}),
\]
All corresponding effective time points, durations, and event orders represent the same process. Event totals and dimensionless intensities are unchanged; rates and durations still transform in opposite directions under [17.7](#sec-17-7).

If only the real-world time unit/origin changes by \(t'=bt+d,\ b>0\), while abstract units remain fixed, then
\(T_E'=bT_E,\ T_M'=bT_M,\ t_0'=bt_0+d\),
and \(r_T\) and both abstract trajectories remain unchanged. Changing only \(T_E\) or \(T_M\) without consistently changing the corresponding dynamics, inputs, and event timing generally changes the actual process; it is not a pure unit conversion. These are mathematical identities, with no real-clock data substituted.

<a id="sec-15"></a>

## 15 Output Quantization and State-Persistence Boundaries

<a id="sec-15-1"></a>

### 15.1 Final Output

The final output contains eleven values:
\[
y=(y_J,y_I,y_C,y_R,y_F,y_A,y_S,y_D,y_H,y_G,y_{\rm Fat}),\qquad
y^{\rm emo}=(y_i)_{i\in\mathcal I}.
\]
\(m,e,s,\theta\), goal motivation, and work effort are not expanded into additional output axes. The first ten values are mood intensities; the eleventh is a subjective mental-fatigue proxy. Mood, fatigue, and actual capability have distinct semantics. The output supplies state values, which memory stores with time under [17.12](#sec-17-12). The output itself contains no behavioral rules for independently refusing tasks, changing memory policies, or changing permissions because of fatigue.

<a id="sec-15-2"></a>

### 15.2 Three-Decimal Quantization Preserving Spatial Constraints

Direct rounding to the nearest value may cross the admissible-region boundary. To prevent this, define:

\[
K_i^{\min}=\lceil1000L_i(m)\rceil,
\]

\[
K_i^{\max}=\lfloor1000U_i(m)\rfloor.
\]

Require:

\[
1-\ell_{\max}\ge0.001.
\]

This ensures that every admissible interval contains at least one grid point at thousandth-unit spacing.

First compute:

\[
K_i^{\rm near}=\lfloor1000x_i+0.5\rfloor,
\]

Then define:

\[
\boxed{
y_i=
\frac{
\operatorname{clip}_{[K_i^{\min},K_i^{\max}]}
(K_i^{\rm near})
}{1000}.
}
\]

Therefore:

\[
y_i\in\{0,0.001,\ldots,1\},
\]

\[
y^{\rm emo}\in\mathcal C(m),
\]

\[
|y_i-x_i|<0.001.
\]

For fatigue, use ordinary nearest-grid-point quantization:
\[
\boxed{y_{\rm Fat}=\frac{\operatorname{clip}_{[0,1000]}
(\lfloor1000f+0.5\rfloor)}{1000}},\qquad
|y_{\rm Fat}-f|\le0.0005.
\]
Thus the complete output is \(y\in(\mathcal C(m)\times[0,1])\cap\{0,.001,\ldots,1\}^{11}\). Do not apply an emotion's \(m_i,\ell_i\) to fatigue: long-term joy or sadness must not impose unsupported upper or lower fatigue bounds.

All output values have at most three decimal places. Internal quantities are not subject to this precision limit. This section defines exact real arithmetic. Finite-precision implementations must follow [16.3.1](#sec-16-3-1), without assuming that each floating-point multiplication, addition, ceil, or floor equals its exact counterpart.

<a id="sec-15-3"></a>

### 15.3 No Writeback of Quantized Values

The next state evolution uses:

\[
(b,s,m,e,w,f,Q_E,P^E,\Xi_E,D_E),
\]

rather than \(y\).

Internal changes smaller than \(0.001\) can therefore accumulate gradually instead of being erased by rounding at every output.

<a id="sec-15-4"></a>

### 15.4 Information Lost Through Compression

The output discards:

- Object-specific sentiments.
- Affective-style adaptation, current effort / compensation goals, recovery quality, and emotion-side memory-replay budgets.
- The decomposition into long-term background and proximal response.
- Appraisal content and reliability.
- Internal states required for future evolution.
- Precision below one thousandth.

Consequently, **the eleven-axis output is not a complete restorable state**. Supporting suspension and restoration requires saving the full internal state and continuous-input registry, not just eleven output numbers.

The auxiliary fatigue output is generated from the internal full-precision state; the next step does not reconstruct state from the quantized value. Existing consumers accepting only ten axes must explicitly upgrade their interface version. They must not silently discard the eleventh axis while claiming to implement the complete expression interface specified here.

<a id="sec-16"></a>

## 16 Computation Order and Numerical Implementation

<a id="sec-16-1"></a>

### 16.1 Semantic Commit Order

1. Validate version, abstract time, evidence/event IDs, finalized, masks, cause scopes, permissions, memory budgets, effort-source enumeration and eligibility, and order at shared times. If the coupled interface is enabled, also validate the shared-time contract, field units, and correspondence within the shared batch.
2. Advance continuous states to the next event's \(\tau^-\), splitting at mode changes, source changes, and budget exhaustion. Update ongoing \(\dot D_l^{\rm exp}=a_l^{\rm exp}\) and \(\dot I_{li}^{\rm exp}=a_l^{\rm exp}e_i\).
3. In semantic order, fix the current event's pre-event snapshot, appraisal, and once-saturated dose; solve the emotion / object-learning map in [11.2](#sec-11-2) or [11.4](#sec-11-4).
4. Atomically commit the event, deduplication, and memory consumption; update tasks/concerns and register eligible experience under [9.4](#sec-9-4). This step does not make \(b,m,f\) jump.
5. If the event is associated with a normal new memory and uses a post_event snapshot, first perform a pure read of the current state under [17.12](#sec-17-12) and generate a time-tagged emotion envelope. This adds no emotional jump, dose, or load. Commit memory storage in the shared causal order. For later events at the same time, repeat steps 3–4 and their required snapshots without advancing \(\tau\); do not silently reuse the snapshot from before the entire batch.
6. Update right-continuous activity, attention, and regulation-target inputs. Under [9.3.4](#sec-9-3-4), atomically process override acceptance, rejection, expiry, and capacity conflicts, selecting in priority order: suspension, effective override, automatic policy. Compute \(C_f,u_{\rm reg}\) from current \(f\), then \(u_\Sigma,\mathcal L,\mathcal R_f,K\). Separately compute \(u_E\) from task/recall effort. Reconstruct \(x\) and output the eleven-axis \(y\) under [Section 15](#sec-15).

If fixed-encoding pure stimuli may be merged, \(e,w\) uses the common flow with \(\Theta_i=\sum\vartheta_{ri}\). Evidence, sentiments, and experience profiles still follow their own contracts. This optimization does not make arbitrary complete-state updates order-independent. Continuous layers evolve simultaneously, not through different psychological times in program order.

<a id="sec-16-2"></a>

### 16.2 Interval-Preserving Continuous Approximation

Interval preservation does not guarantee fixed-step stability. Step-size, finite-precision, and error criteria are in [Appendix C](#sec-c).

For \(\dot z=a(q-z)\) and \(a\ge0\), freeze coefficients at the start of the step and use
\(z_{n+1}=e^{-ah}z_n+(1-e^{-ah})q_n\); when \(a=0\), leave the value unchanged.
The nonnegative rates and convex targets for \(m,w,s\) are formed directly from the equations.

For \(e\), at the start of the step let \(K'_i=K_i\mathbf1[e_i>m_i]\) and
\(D'_i=D_i^{\rm att}\mathbf1[e_i>T_i]\), and take
\[
a_i=\lambda_{e,i}+K'_i+D'_i,\qquad
q_i=\frac{(\lambda_{e,i}+D'_i)T_i+K'_im_i}{a_i}\in[0,1].
\]
This frozen formula is consistent with the current vector field and preserves the interval; it is not claimed to be exact over a whole step that crosses a positive-part branch. For fatigue, take
\(a=a_f\mathcal L+b_f\mathcal R_f\). When \(a>0\), use \(q=a_f\mathcal L/a\in[0,1]\); when \(a=0\), retain \(f\). This step freezes actual effort and recovery quantities. Changes in automatic compensation with \(f\) still require synchronous recomputation as the step size is refined.

For L1, freeze \(a_E^c,j_E,z^E\), set \(k=a_E^c+\lambda_D+j_E/Q_{\max}>0\), and let \(A_h=e^{-kh}\):
\[
Q_E^{n+1}=A_hQ_E^n+(1-A_h)j_E/k,
\]
\[
P_i^{E,n+1}=A_hP_i^{E,n}+(1-A_h)j_Ez_i^E/k.
\]
The two targets satisfy \(0\le j_Ez_i^E/k\le j_E/k\le Q_{\max}\), so every summary bound is preserved.

Along the exact linear path within the frozen step, compute
\[
I_Q=(j_E/k)h+(Q_E^n-j_E/k)(1-A_h)/k,
\]
\[
I_{P_i}=(j_Ez_i^E/k)h+(P_i^{E,n}-j_Ez_i^E/k)(1-A_h)/k.
\]
\[
\Delta\Xi_E=a_E^c I_Q,\qquad
\Delta D_E=(\lambda_D+j_E/Q_{\max})I_Q.
\]
Thus this approximation still satisfies \(Q_E^{n+1}-Q_E^n+\Delta\Xi_E+\Delta D_E=j_Eh\) exactly.

If \(\Delta\Xi_E>0\), define
\[
\bar t_i=(1-\rho_b)b_{0,i}+\rho_bI_{P_i}/I_Q,\qquad
b_i^{n+1}=e^{-\Delta\Xi_E/H_b}b_i^n+
(1-e^{-\Delta\Xi_E/H_b})\bar t_i;
\]
Otherwise \(b\) is unchanged. For a general time-varying target, this is a first-order approximation preserving \(\mathcal B\), not an exact complete solution. All continuous right-hand sides share the state at the start of the step; program order must not substitute for psychological causality.

<a id="sec-16-3"></a>

### 16.3 Numerical Event Representation and Convergence Scope

For stimulate, \(e(\theta)\) has a closed-form solution, and \(w\) is integrated along the same \(\theta\) path. An exponential convex update with frozen target \(\max(F_i(e^n,m),w_i^n)\) may be used. reassess uses a convex update for \(F_i(e^n,m)\). Sentiments have the standard linear exponential solution; the experience-registration map is itself closed-form.

With fixed modes, piecewise-Lipschitz inputs, a finite interval, and alignment to discontinuities, the consistent first-order methods above converge as step size tends to zero. For merely measurable inputs, their integrals must be approximated consistently; arbitrary start-of-step sampling does not establish convergence. Partition and commutation results for exact flows do not imply exact equality for arbitrary coarse-step numerical methods. Domain preservation also does not imply small error.

Align steps with budget-exhaustion boundaries; [Section 13](#sec-13) may be used for long purely passive gaps. A large frozen step does not replace general time-varying coupling. Numerical steps create no new experience, fatigue impulses, replay, or events.

<a id="sec-16-3-1"></a>

#### 16.3.1 Exact Integer Representation of Grid Quantization and Numerical Error

Using \(U_i=1-\ell_i(1-m_i)\) and
\(\lfloor N-z\rfloor=N-\lceil z\rceil\) (integer \(N\)), the two bounds in [15.2](#sec-15-2) can equivalently be written as
\[
K_i^{\min}=\lceil1000\ell_i m_i\rceil,\qquad
K_i^{\max}=1000-\lceil1000\ell_i(1-m_i)\rceil.
\]
Since \(\ell_i>0\), exact arithmetic also gives
\[
K_i^{\min}=
\max\{\lceil1000\ell_i m_i\rceil,\mathbf1[m_i>0]\},
\]
\[
K_i^{\max}=1000-
\max\{\lceil1000\ell_i(1-m_i)\rceil,\mathbf1[m_i<1]\}.
\]
The indicator terms are redundant in exact arithmetic. They can prevent some floating-point endpoint underflow or cancellation, but cannot alone guarantee correct multiplication and rounding near every interior grid point. If a positive product is slightly greater than an integer, rounding it to that integer before ceil still produces a result one grid step too low.

**Computable exact contract.** The input is the currently stored admissible finite-precision state. Treat its binary or decimal values as exact rational numbers, without claiming they equal the exact theoretical trajectory. Let
\[
\ell_i=a/b,\qquad m_i=c/d,\qquad
a,b,c,d\in\mathbb Z,\ b,d>0,\quad
0<a<b,\ 0\le c\le d.
\]
Define the integer operators
\[
\operatorname{cdiv}(n,q)=
\left\lfloor\frac{n+q-1}{q}\right\rfloor,
\quad n\in\mathbb Z_{\ge0},\ q\in\mathbb Z_{>0}.
\]
Then
\[
\boxed{
K_i^{\min}=\operatorname{cdiv}(1000ac,bd),\quad
K_i^{\max}=1000-\operatorname{cdiv}(1000a(d-c),bd).
}
\]
Reconstruct
\(x_i=\ell_i m_i+(1-\ell_i)w_i=v/q\)
from the exact rational values of the stored \(\ell_i,m_i,w_i\), obtaining
\[
K_i^{\rm near}=
\left\lfloor\frac{2000v+q}{2q}\right\rfloor.
\]
Apply the same nearest-grid-point formula to the stored fatigue \(f=v_f/q_f\). All multiplication, addition, division, and comparisons above use integer arithmetic without overflow. Serialize only the thousandth-unit value corresponding to integer \(K\) at the end. Every finite floating-point value has a finite rational representation, so this is a fully computable representation; it does not require solving the entire ODE with rational arithmetic.

Proven interval arithmetic or directed rounding may also be used. Commit only when every candidate value in the interval yields the same integer index; otherwise increase precision or use the exact rational formulas above. At an exact boundary, repeated interval narrowing need not terminate; the exact integer formulas provide a fallback definition for finite inputs. An unspecified epsilon must not modify ceil/floor, nor may a “very small positive number” be treated as zero to obtain a desired reading.

**Reference quantities for error.** If \(\widehat m,\widehat w\) is an approximate continuous solution, the quantization guarantee is relative to \(\mathcal C(\widehat m)\) and the \(\widehat x\) reconstructed from those numerical values. With the same fixed \(\ell\),
\[
|\widehat x_i-x_i|
\le\ell_i|\widehat m_i-m_i|
+(1-\ell_i)|\widehat w_i-w_i|.
\]
so
\[
|y_i-x_i|<
0.001+\ell_i|\widehat m_i-m_i|
+(1-\ell_i)|\widehat w_i-w_i|.
\]
Exact quantization relative to an approximate box is not an error-free guarantee relative to the unknown box of the exact trajectory. If integration or storage has already rounded a positive state to zero, the quantizer cannot recover that lost information; higher precision or explicit approximation-error control is required. [Appendix B.13](#sec-b-13) does not authorize changes to the internal recovery equations or introduction of a psychological dead zone.

This representation equals [15.2](#sec-15-2) in exact arithmetic and preserves the distinction between three-decimal output and accumulation of small internal changes. It adds no output axis or emotion-update mechanism. Absence of rounding differences on one particular trajectory does not guarantee the same for other admissible states. Runtime on a particular language, precision, and hardware platform is not a universal cost bound for this specification. Arbitrary-precision integer costs depend on bit length; a fixed number of axes does not imply constant time independent of precision.

<a id="sec-16-4"></a>

### 16.4 Complete Persistence and Restoration

Complete restoration data must retain the model-version identifier `v1.17`, time units, parameters, \(b_0,b,s,m,e,w,f,Q_E,P^E,\Xi_E,D_E\), sources and cause scopes, complete effort and goal overrides, regulation-effort provenance categories and override-validity intervals, \(q_{\rm rest},\chi,g_E\) with provenance labels, simultaneous-event order, current accepted-override / expiry / revocation status, evidence deduplication, pending fragments, participation masks of ongoing experiences, \(D_l^{\rm exp},I_{li}^{\rm exp}\) and used fractions, task quality / expectations, concern lifecycles, memory-budget allocation / consumption, fading ownership, and permissions. Reject NaN, Infinity, values outside admissible domains, and incompatible units. \(y\) is not a restoration file. Permanent deduplication must not be claimed after deleting its ledger.

Current personality-integration computation uses a fixed ten-axis summary. Deduplication, auditing, and external-memory storage and retrieval each still have costs; the complete system cannot be described as using entirely fixed memory.

Data compatibility and state import follow [Appendix E](#sec-e), which specifies conditions for complete restoration, missing values, and incomplete summaries.

<a id="sec-17"></a>

## 17 Memory Interface and Shared Definitions

<a id="sec-17-1"></a>

### 17.1 Ownership, Data Encapsulation, and Fields

In normal integration, a memory entry encapsulates content, emotion values, and shared time \(t\). The emotion theory defines the meaning, axis order, scale, and contemporaneous output values of emotion fields. The memory theory stores those values with content and defines memory formation, persistence, retrieval, and replay. Each theory retains its own states, equations, and input contracts. Shared data definitions do not require merging them into a single theory.

The specified data directions include:
\[
\text{snapshot of emotion values and time}\ \longrightarrow\ \text{context of a memory entry},
\]
\[
\text{memory content and historical context}\ \longrightarrow\ \text{current emotional appraisal/response}.
\]
[17.12–17.14](#sec-17-12) and [Appendices F.2–F.3](#sec-f-2) define envelope recording, shared definitions, use of readings, and their applicability conditions. A complete internal memory theory has not been supplied; adding a recording direction does not establish completed integration.

**Direct update authority is distinct from data dependence.** Let \(M_{\rm ext}\) be the external memory state, \(\Theta_{\rm mem}\) its policy/rule parameters, and \(Z_{\rm emo}\) the complete emotion state. The emotion module's own event/personality-learning operators still satisfy
\[
\mathcal U_{\rm emo}(Z_{\rm emo},M_{\rm ext},\Theta_{\rm mem};U)
=\bigl(\mathcal F_{\rm emo}(Z_{\rm emo};U),M_{\rm ext},\Theta_{\rm mem}\bigr),
\]
with direct update contribution
\[
\left.\dot M_{\rm ext}\right|_{\rm emo}=0,\qquad
\left.\dot\Theta_{\rm mem}\right|_{\rm emo}=0.
\]
Emotion data may enter new entries through the memory-side recording operator in [17.12](#sec-17-12), which preserves the emotion-side state and memory-policy parameters. This is consistent with the equations above: personality updates do not directly execute memory writes; memory stores the values it reads through its own defined interface.

A zero direct update contribution does not imply that memory outcomes are independent of emotion values. Personality changes may alter future emotion outputs, and new memory entries may faithfully record those different values. This is not personality learning rewriting memory policies. Nor does this division of responsibilities imply zero dependence of memory equations on emotion data.

The emotion module supplies the definitions for emotional context within memories. If a later memory theory uses that context to determine formation, encoding weights, persistence, or retrieval, it must explicitly specify the corresponding functions, state effects, and psychological basis. This specification reserves that semantic dependence without inventing coefficients or treating it as an established human mechanism. Personality learning still receives no direct authority to modify memory content, weights, or policies.

This chapter's \(B_p\), \(I_p^{\rm mem}\), \(d_{\rm acc}\), and optional fading remain emotion-side accounting or calculations of how much emotional effect a recall injects. They must not automatically be written back as memory-retention periods, retrieval weights, or forgetting rules. Budget exhaustion stops only additional emotional injection charged to that account; it does not prohibit continued memory storage, retrieval, or replay. Historical content is data: old commands and task outcomes must not be treated as new current instructions or outcomes.

Each replay retains replay_id, memory_id, memory_version, original evidence_ids, cause_ids, objects, abstract time point or interval, trigger source, observed and inferred fields, reliability, vividness \(v_{\rm vivid}\in[0,1]\), requested amount \(d_{\rm req}\ge0\), abstract age \(a_{\rm mem}\ge0\), semantic replay count \(n_{\rm replay}\in\mathbb N_0\), fading ownership, and learning eligibility. It also references any available historical emotion envelope and its version. Explicitly mark missing data; unknown emotion must not be filled with zero. External events and replay must not masquerade as one another. Shared time and action identity follow [17.8](#sec-17-8); age and original-time-point conversions follow [17.7](#sec-17-7).

<a id="sec-17-2"></a>

### 17.2 Current Appraisal and Fading

Replay recomputes \(M,a,r_{\rm rel}\) from historical data, current goals, and sentiments; it does not restore the old \(e,m,x\). It does not recount old progress or task closure, nor automatically treat historical threats as current ones. Historical emotion values stored under [17.12](#sec-17-12) describe the context at that time, not the current appraisal, target response, or complete internal state; they cannot be copied directly into current \(e,m,x\). Additional use of historical numerical values in appraisal requires a separately specified valid input transformation and effect function. Current processing uses the established content appraisal; a field's existence alone does not add another stimulus.

If the emotion module owns additional fading:
\[
d_{\rm mod}=d_{\rm req}v_{\rm vivid}
e^{-\mu_pa_{\rm mem}}(q_p^{\rm fade})^{n_{\rm replay}},
\quad\mu_p\ge0,\quad0<q_p^{\rm fade}\le1.
\]
If the memory module owns fading, use only \(d_{\rm req}v_{\rm vivid}\) here. The default is \(\mu_p=0,q_p^{\rm fade}=1\), adding no decay that duplicates the memory module's decay and imposing no faster fading for negative than positive content.

<a id="sec-17-3"></a>

### 17.3 Importance-Dependent Finite Total Budget

At encoding, quantify memory \(p\)'s importance \(I_p^{\rm mem}\in[0,1]\) using the anchors in [5.2](#sec-5-2), according to its effects on the subject's goals, relationships, or persistent consequences, with supporting evidence. Do not infer it backward from emotion intensity after recall. Set:
\[
\boxed{B_p^0=B_{\min}+(B_{\max}-B_{\min})(I_p^{\rm mem})^{\gamma_B},}
\]
\[
0\le B_{\min}\le B_{\max}<\infty,\quad\gamma_B>0.
\]
Irrelevant or unreliable memories need not receive an account, equivalent to \(B_p^0=0\). The formulas above apply to accepted valid memories.

Aliases or memory versions of the same historical evidence share a budget ledger. Only explicit new external evidence can raise importance or add budget. If only importance within the same account is updated, cumulative allocation may increase by at most \( [B(I_{\rm new})-B(I_{\rm old})]_+\), retaining all consumed amounts; the entire account must not be refilled. A genuinely new external event may have its own account, but requires new evidence_ids. Reading, replay, mode changes, rising emotion, and merely becoming more certain do not replenish the budget.

<a id="sec-17-4"></a>

### 17.4 Shared Budget for Instantaneous and Continuous Recall

Instantaneously accepted amount and effective dose:
\[
d_{\rm offer}=S_d(d_{\rm mod}),\quad
d_{\rm acc}=\min(d_{\rm offer},B_p^-),\quad
B_p^+=B_p^--d_{\rm acc},
\]
\[
d_r=d_{\rm acc},\qquad \xi_r=d_{\rm acc}q_{{\rm evt},r}r_{{\rm rel},r}.
\]
Do not apply \(S_d\) again in [Section 11](#sec-11). Merge fragments of the same replay before saturation. A genuinely new replay occurrence may request another allocation, but consumes the same account.

Continuous recall supplies the effective source \(v_p^{\rm rep}\) after attention allocation, subject to the intensity cap in [Section 10](#sec-10), and consumes budget at
\[
\dot B_p=-\zeta_Bv_p^{\rm rep},\qquad\zeta_B>0
\]
Thus, without new allocations:
\[
\sum_k d_{{\rm acc},k}+
\int_0^\infty\zeta_Bv_p^{\rm rep}(\tau)d\tau\le B_p^0.
\]
Injection stops at zero budget; a negative balance followed by correction is prohibited. The original memory is not deleted. If one recall uses both instantaneous and continuous representations, explicitly allocate budget and load between them; each representation must not use the full amount.

The budget controls total injection, not the number of possible recalls or the duration of emotion. Initial values allow larger budgets for important memories, but do not assert that major loss should subside after a particular number of real-world hours. Persistent consequences are driven by the concerns in [Section 13](#sec-13); in that case, the cessation-of-nonreplay-sources premise in [Appendix B.5](#sec-b-5) does not hold.

<a id="sec-17-5"></a>

### 17.5 Learning Isolation and Familiarity

For replay alone, use \(q^{\rm learn}=0\) and allocate no new experience fraction \(c_l\). New reflection requires an independent inference_id and a new conclusion before its experience eligibility can be assessed under [9.4](#sec-9-4). Stronger emotion alone cannot increase direct evidence about an object. Recall may elicit responses on several axes; memories are not permanently bound to a single emotion.

Optional familiarity \(\phi_{\rm fam}\in[0,1]\), \(0\le\rho_h<1\):
\[
n_{\rm eff}=n(1-\phi_{\rm fam}),\qquad
d_{\rm req,eff}=d_{\rm req}(1-\rho_h\phi_{\rm fam}).
\]
Apply it before saturation and through only one owner; the default is \(\rho_h=0\). Familiarity is not deduplication. An event that actually occurs again is not deleted merely because it is similar.

A finite budget is a conservative engineering condition and may underestimate recurrent recollection over many years. If external scheduling depends on \(x\) and closes a feedback loop, the corresponding guarantees hold only under the conditions of [Appendix A](#sec-a) and [Appendix B.5](#sec-b-5). This specification does not claim that arbitrary memory feedback loops have no rumination attractors.

<a id="sec-17-6"></a>

### 17.6 Shared Real-World Time and Independent Abstract Timescales for the Same Subject

When emotion and memory theories are subsequently integrated, normal operation shares elapsed real-world time \(t\), a time unit, and effective event-time semantics. This section concerns the same modeled subject; shared time does not imply equal psychological rates in the modules. At this stage, \(t\) remains symbolic: no dates, real timestamps, or system-clock readings are consumed.

Let \(t_0\) be a shared reference time point. Denote the emotion module's original \(\tau\) by \(\tau_E\), and memory's abstract time by \(\tau_M\). Choose finite positive reference timescales
\[
T_E:=T_{\rm ref}>0,\qquad T_M>0
\]
and known reference values \(\tau_{E0},\tau_{M0}\), and define
\[
\boxed{
\tau_E(t)=\tau_{E0}+\frac{t-t_0}{T_E},\qquad
\tau_M(t)=\tau_{M0}+\frac{t-t_0}{T_M}.
}
\]
The two \(T\) use the same unit of real-world duration; each layer's decay rates remain defined in its own abstract time. \(T_M\) is a time-interface parameter of the subsequent memory theory. This specification assigns it no real-world numerical value and does not treat \(T_M=T_E\) as a calibrated fact.

Using emotion time as the shared analysis coordinate, let
\[
\tau_c:=\tau_E-\tau_{E0},\qquad r_T:=T_E/T_M>0.
\]
Then
\[
\tau_E=\tau_{E0}+\tau_c,\qquad
\tau_M=\tau_{M0}+r_T\tau_c.
\]
\(\tau_c\) is only a coordinate for the same process, not a third psychological process. Nor is it the event-flow length \(\vartheta\), dose progress \(\sigma\), or conversation-turn count of [Section 11](#sec-11). The modules have equal abstract-time readings only if they choose the same reference timescale and origin. Different origins or scales do not prevent sharing \(t\).

If the memory theory is already written directly in shared \(t\) as
\(dX_M/dt=G_M(t,X_M,U_M)\), no additional psychological clock is needed. Its equivalent abstract representation is
\[
F_M(\tau_M,Z_M,U_M)
=T_MG_M\!\left(t_0+T_M(\tau_M-\tau_{M0}),Z_M,U_M\right).
\]
This is a coordinate change. Substitution into the shared equation gives
\(dZ_M/d\tau_c=T_EG_M\), so an arbitrary choice of memory abstract-time scale does not change the actual process.

Once both local abstract equations and their parameters are fully specified, specifying a positive ratio \(r_T\) makes the coupled equations computable entirely in abstract \(\tau_c\), without first calibrating real-world minutes. If memory supplies only \(G_M\) with real-time coefficients, compatible abstract coefficients or the required timescale must still be provided; a ratio alone does not determine every numerical value. These conditions establish computability, not human validity of the ratio or conversion. An unspecified ratio yields a family of coupled processes parameterized by \(r_T\), not a unique numerical trajectory.

The coordinates of a shared effective event \(t_k\) satisfy
\[
\tau_{E,k}-\tau_{E0}
=(\tau_{M,k}-\tau_{M0})/r_T
=(t_k-t_0)/T_E.
\]
Map any continuous interval \([t_a,t_b)\) and its endpoints in the same way, preserving half-open semantics. Message counts, memory-retrieval counts, numerical substeps, and program-completion order do not supply \(t_k\) or elapsed intervals.

A module receiving no new messages is distinct from its clock stopping. Memory may set certain state derivatives to zero in modes permitted by its theory, while emotion recovers according to its own modes; both remain on the same \(t\). Suspending active activity, the user's departure, or a computation not yet being executed does not automatically rewrite the time mapping. Every suspension/resumption mode must explicitly specify input and state evolution; skipping computational steps is not a psychological mechanism.

<a id="sec-17-7"></a>

### 17.7 Intermodule Units, Age, and Replay Budgets

In addition to its value domain, each numerical field must declare whether it is a state/intensity, event total, rate per unit time, duration, or time point, together with its owner and time basis. Conversion from \(\tau_M\) to \(\tau_E\) follows:

| Type | Conversion rule | Constraint |
|---|---|---|
| Dimensionless state, reliability, vividness, and attention fraction | Value unchanged; reparameterize only at the same time point | Do not amplify intensity by the scale ratio |
| Single-event total dose \(d\), experience fraction, or allocated budget | Value unchanged | Do not multiply by the time ratio or allocate again per transmission fragment |
| Rate \(\varrho_M^{\rm qty}\) of the same agreed quantity per unit of memory time | \(\varrho_E^{\rm qty}=r_T\varrho_M^{\rm qty}\) | Preserve \(\varrho_E^{\rm qty}\,d\tau_E=\varrho_M^{\rm qty}\,d\tau_M\) |
| Duration or age \(a_M\) on the memory timescale | \(a_E=a_M/r_T\) | Must represent the same real-world period; renaming a field is insufficient |
| Time point | \(\tau_E=\tau_{E0}+(\tau_M-\tau_{M0})/r_T\) | Preserve origin, effective-time type, and event order |

These rules convert time units for the same agreed quantity; they do not equate different constructs. If amount units or operational definitions differ between modules, an explicit additional mapping is required. Memory's own learning amount cannot directly serve as effective experience for emotion L1. Nor do these rules mistake source intensity for dose rate. For example, \(v_p^{\rm rep}\) is an already normalized effective source intensity on the emotion side, read at the same time point without multiplication by \(r_T\) for different clock scales. Emotion-side consumption is still defined by \(\zeta_B\).

The chapter's \(a_{\rm mem}\) is explicitly memory age in emotion-time units:
\[
a_{\rm mem}=a_E
=\frac{t-t_{\rm origin}}{T_E}
=\frac{T_M}{T_E}a_M.
\]
\(t_{\rm origin}\) is the historical origin agreed for that age field, not the current transmission or replay time. The memory theory must specify the origin type; retransmission does not reset it. If only an admissible memory-scale age and its reference time point are known, the same conversion applies without a real date. Insufficient data must not be filled with zero or described as a known age; reject the field or explicitly mark it not yet computable according to its contract.

If the emotion side owns fading, then
\[
e^{-\mu_p a_{\rm mem}}
=e^{-(\mu_p/T_E)(t-t_{\rm origin})}.
\]
Thus \(\mu_p/T_E\) is the corresponding real-time fading rate; \(T_E\) and \(\mu_p\) must not be conflated. If memory already owns that same fading, retain the single-owner rule of [17.2](#sec-17-2) and do not multiply by another decay factor.

In shared time, the continuous-recall ledger is
\[
\frac{dB_p}{dt}=-\frac{\zeta_B}{T_E}v_p^{\rm rep}(t),\qquad
\frac{dB_p}{d\tau_c}=-\zeta_Bv_p^{\rm rep}(\tau_c).
\]
Representing the same ledger in memory time gives
\[
\frac{dB_p}{d\tau_M}=-\frac{\zeta_B}{r_T}v_p^{\rm rep}.
\]
Therefore, without new allocation,
\[
\sum_k d_{{\rm acc},k}
+\int \zeta_Bv_p^{\rm rep}\,d\tau_E
=\sum_k d_{{\rm acc},k}
+\int \frac{\zeta_B}{T_E}v_p^{\rm rep}\,dt
\le B_p^0,
\]
The integrals cover the same complete usage period. This equality follows directly by change of variables. Changing time units cannot create budget; exhaustion still stops only additional emotional injection.

Shared time also does not duplicate load. The same actual recall activity supplies one \(u_{\rm rec}\) fraction under the same activity_id; memory-side execution and emotion-side receipt must not each be charged. Additional emotion-regulation effort is still measured by \(u_{\rm reg}\). Internal memory maintenance or software computational costs enter fatigue inputs only when they satisfy the subject's existing activity/effort contract; they are not automatically subjective mental effort. If memory also has quantities named fatigue, familiarity, or importance, first distinguish their operational definitions and owners. Equal names do not authorize addition, mutual overwriting, or duplicate application of the same effect.

<a id="sec-17-8"></a>

### 17.8 Shared Events, Causal Order, and Exactly-Once Effects

The coupled interface uses a single time contract retaining at least:
a shared timeline_id, time-contract version, symbolic or numerical definitions of \(t_0,\tau_{E0},\tau_{M0},T_E,T_M\), the specified \(r_T\), a shared identifier for each effective time point and its \(\tau_c\), local semantic order, source module, source-state version, root event/evidence ID, occurrence_id, payload version, and effect type. These are interface data, not additional emotion-output axes. Real timestamps are not required at this stage.

Distinguish the original historical occurrence time, the effective time when the subject now acquires or recalls the content, and the program's receipt or computation-completion time. An emotional jump acts at the shared effective time of the subject's current acquisition. A memory's historical origin cannot rewrite past emotion, and software completion order cannot silently change psychological order. The theoretical default aligns both modules to the supplied effective timing. Any additional nonzero psychological transmission delay requires an explicitly specified delay and necessary history; it must not be inferred automatically from computational latency.

At the same effective time point, first advance both modules' continuous states to their corresponding left limits, then update through one finite semantic batch. The batch must preserve:

- The local precedence already specified by each module's theory, including [Section 11](#sec-11), [16.1](#sec-16-1), and [9.3.4](#sec-9-3-4) on the emotion side.
- Placement of an event generated from a committed output after that source commit.
- The distinction between processing the same original evidence and a genuinely new replay occurrence.
- Existing boundary semantics for source starts/ends, override expiry, and mode changes.

Represent these precedence constraints by a directed acyclic graph. Specify a fixed strict total order tie_order covering every node, with unique identifiers as tie breakers. Repeatedly choosing an executable node by tie_order yields the deterministic topological order selected by this contract; the graph itself need not have a unique topological ordering. Cyclic constraints make the batch nonconforming. Thread speed must not arbitrarily choose a result. Events with no precedence still use the declared engineering order, which is not called human psychological time.

One explicit sufficient condition excluding infinitely many returns at the same time is finite branching from every root event and an integer rank that strictly decreases along each same-time derived event, with lower bound zero. Root events must also be locally finite in every finite interval of shared time. Each derived-event tree is then finite, as is the union of finitely many such trees. This is an available engineering sufficient condition only. A later memory theory using another event-generation form must provide a joint non-Zeno condition rather than merely citing separate results for the modules.

Uniquely identify each effect by
\[
\mathrm{effect\_id}
=(\mathrm{occurrence\_id},\mathrm{target\_module},\mathrm{effect\_role})
\]
and retain the original evidence_ids separately. For replay, occurrence_id corresponds to replay_id; retransmission of the same replay must not create a new value. Memory storing an event and emotion appraising it may be distinct valid effects of the same root event. Sharing a root ID neither deletes one effect nor creates an additional emotional stimulus. A later recall has a new occurrence_id but still references old evidence_ids; under [17.5](#sec-17-5), it does not automatically add sentiments evidence or personality-experience fractions.

If \(C_{\rm done}\) is the set of committed effects and the effect map \(\mathcal J_d\) is single-valued and preserves the admissible domain, define deduplicated commitment by
\[
\widehat{\mathcal J}_d(Z,C_{\rm done})=
\begin{cases}
(Z,C_{\rm done}),&d\in C_{\rm done},\\
(\mathcal J_d(Z),C_{\rm done}\cup\{d\}),&d\notin C_{\rm done}.
\end{cases}
\]
Retransmission with the same ID and payload is the identity map. The same ID with a different payload is a contract error. Emotion-budget consumption, deduplication, and psychological effects are still committed atomically under [11.5](#sec-11-5). This formula gives emotion/personality updates no direct write authority over memory policies. Emotion snapshots and memory-side envelope commits have distinct effect_role values and are stored through the memory-side operator specified in [17.12](#sec-17-12). Recording and acknowledgement alone do not create additional emotional effects.

Within-batch ordering indices, acknowledgements, and retransmissions do not advance \(t,\tau_E,\tau_M\) or add dose or load. If numerical formats yield different approximations to the same nominal time point, use the known shared-time identifier and encoding contract. Arbitrary epsilon values must not merge psychologically distinct events. Time fields also follow the stored-value and textual-representation semantics of [Appendix C.5](#sec-c-5).

<a id="sec-17-9"></a>

### 17.9 Coupled Equations and Existence-and-Uniqueness Conditions

A complete, verifiable specification of the subsequent memory theory has not been supplied in this project. This section represents it through an explicit mathematical interface and states the required conditions without inventing its internal mechanisms. It does not establish completed integration or a joint proof of both theories.

Let \(Z_E\) comprise all emotion states and necessary accounting quantities participating in evolution under this specification, with domain \(\mathcal D_E\) from [Appendix A](#sec-a). Let \(Z_M\in\mathcal D_M\) be the memory theory's own complete state. That theory must supply at least the following traceable items:

| Item | Required mathematical specification |
|---|---|
| Independent state and initialization | \(\mathcal D_M,Z_M(\tau_{M0})\), its own parameters and modes, and which counters may grow without bound but remain finite on finite intervals |
| Continuous evolution | \(dZ_M/d\tau_M=F_M(\tau_M,Z_M,U_M)\), admissible input set, solution concept, and continuation conditions |
| Events and boundaries | Single-valued jumps \(\mathcal J_M\), timing and generation rules, simultaneous-event order, domain preservation, and non-Zeno conditions |
| External output | Fields, domains, units, historical and current time points, data versions, observed/inferred status, and fading/budget ownership for \(H_M\) |
| Dependencies and permissions | Normal entries read emotion/time envelopes under [17.12](#sec-17-12). Other reads, usage functions, recording triggers, and shared fields must still be specified, separately from external parameter updates |

Let \(F_E\) denote this specification's continuous equations and \(\mathcal A_{ME}\) the read-only adapter from memory output to valid emotion input. On continuous intervals without capture/storage events, this specification adds no instantaneous feedthrough from current emotion to memory, so write
\[
U_E=\mathcal A_{ME}(\tau_c,Z_E,H_M(Z_M),U^{\rm ext}),
\qquad U_M=U_M^{\rm ext};
\]
Here \(H_M(Z_M)\) is shorthand; any additional dependence of the output on \(\tau_M,U_M^{\rm ext}\) must also be included explicitly. Memory's own defined internal effects may be included in \(F_M,H_M\). Committed historical emotion envelopes are data in \(Z_M\), not excluded inputs. Emotion-to-memory capture/storage is specified by the event maps in [17.12](#sec-17-12). The equations above therefore describe only interval flows, not a unidirectional complete hybrid system. If memory later requires continuous input from current emotion, add the function under [17.10](#sec-17-10) and reanalyze the actual dependencies under [Appendix F.3](#sec-f-3). \(U^{\rm ext}\) summarizes existing valid external inputs only; it is not an additional emotion variable.

In shared real-world time, write
\(X_E(t)=Z_E(\tau_E(t))\) and \(X_M(t)=Z_M(\tau_M(t))\). The chain rule gives
\[
\frac{dX_E}{dt}=\frac1{T_E}F_E(\tau_E(t),X_E,U_E),\qquad
\frac{dX_M}{dt}=\frac1{T_M}F_M(\tau_M(t),X_M,U_M).
\]
The abstract coupled representation used at this stage is
\[
\boxed{
\frac{dZ_E}{d\tau_c}
=F_E(\tau_{E0}+\tau_c,Z_E,U_E),\qquad
\frac{dZ_M}{d\tau_c}
=r_TF_M(\tau_{M0}+r_T\tau_c,Z_M,U_M).
}
\]
where \(Z_E,Z_M\) are all regarded as functions of the shared coordinate. Computation is possible once the component equations, adapter, initial conditions, ratio, and event contract are specified. Calling each module once does not advance each by an independently fixed duration.

**Conditional proposition.** Assume:

1. Within each mode, the continuous state has fixed finite dimension. New objects or dimensions are handled by locally finite event maps with explicit initialization and new domains. In each fixed mode, both vector fields are locally Lipschitz in their states and continuous inputs, measurable in time, and have local Lipschitz constants and local vector-field bounds integrable on finite time intervals. Output \(H_M\) and the continuous parts of the adapter are measurable in time; their local Lipschitz constants are uniformly bounded on each finite interval, and their ranges lie in the modules' admissible input domains. Relevant local vector-field bounds cover those input values. Relaxing uniform boundedness requires a separate proof that the composed constants are integrable; integrability of individual factors does not imply integrability of their product.
2. For every dependency in the equations, inputs are explicitly determined by current states and defined external data. There is no undefined algebraic loop. Discrete classifications, masks, and semantic entries are handled by specified events or modes, not treated as continuous Lipschitz quantities.
3. The complete coupled event sequence is deterministic and locally finite, or its generation rules separately guarantee unique selection, finite simultaneous batches, and non-Zeno behavior. Event maps are single-valued and preserve admissible domains, with ordering as in [17.8](#sec-17-8).
4. Both modules satisfy domain-preservation and finite-time continuation conditions over the stated input range. Compact invariant psychological/memory-state domains together with accounting quantities bounded on finite intervals may provide this guarantee. Similar module names do not establish this property for the other module.

Then the coupled system has a unique hybrid trajectory on every finite interval of shared time and preserves
\(\mathcal D_E\times\mathcal D_M\). If a mode change changes state dimension, the product domain refers to the domain specified for each mode; intermode embeddings and initialization belong to the explicitly specified event maps.

**Proof.** Lipschitz properties of states and adapters compose on admissible local regions. For example, let \(L_{Ez},L_{Eu},L_{Mz}\) denote bounds for the local vector fields with respect to state/emotion input, and let \(A_E,A_M\) denote the composed adapter's bounds with respect to \(Z_E,Z_M\), all evaluated in shared \(\tau_c\). For the interface above, with no instantaneous emotion feedthrough within the interval and identical external input, a joint Lipschitz bound under the sum of the two state norms is
\[
\Lambda_c
=L_{Ez}+L_{Eu}(A_E+A_M)+r_TL_{Mz}.
\]
By the assumed boundedness and integrability, \(\Lambda_c\) is integrable, as are the local vector-field bounds. The finite positive coefficient \(r_T\) only scales the second vector field and does not change this property. Every event-free mode interval therefore has a unique Carathéodory solution. Each module preserves its own domain for every admissible input from the other, so the product domain is invariant; finite-time continuation conditions exclude escape within the interval. At each effective time point, apply single-valued maps in the finite deterministic order to obtain a unique right-side state, then continue to the next interval. Non-Zeno behavior ensures finitely many concatenations in finite time. This proves the proposition.

The proposition requires conditions on the **joint input range and joint event generation**. Separate validity of each module for some external inputs does not make arbitrary connections satisfy these conditions. Deterministic jumps at fixed times may yield unique concatenation even when discontinuous. However, applying the Lipschitz error bound in [Appendix C.7](#sec-c-7) additionally requires jump-sensitivity bounds; existence and uniqueness do not replace them.

This is a deterministic conditional proposition without additional transmission delay. If a future memory theory contains stochastic forcing, genuine time delays, or unhandled state-dependent switching, it must provide the corresponding probabilistic/history data, solution concept, and joint applicability conditions. They are not implicitly treated as the present ODE.

**Preservation of emotion-side bounds.** As long as the adapter supplies admissible inputs, [Appendix A](#sec-a) continues to preserve emotion-side value domains, the fatigue interval, the personality-anchoring domain, and
\[
x_i=\ell_i m_i+(1-\ell_i)w_i\in\mathcal C(m)
\]
Multiplication by a positive rate in shared time does not reverse inward boundary directions. Memory events do not directly jump internal emotion states; they act only through the specified emotion event maps. [Section 15](#sec-15) still outputs the same eleven axes, with unchanged range and decimal-place rules.

<a id="sec-17-10"></a>

### 17.10 Recording Direction and an Optional Hold Interface

This specification defines the emotion-to-memory envelope-recording direction: emotion outputs snapshots with time and semantic version, and memory stores them under [17.12](#sec-17-12). The memory-to-emotion replay/recall direction is also retained. This is the data contract for normal integration, not an already connected implementation. Personality learning still has zero direct update effect on memory policies; recording emotion data does not mean that personality learning rewrites policy parameters.

Distinguish three levels: defined data storage; the memory theory's future use of those data; and properties of the complete feedback loop after that use is introduced. This specification establishes the first level and the conditions required for the third. Encoding, retrieval, retention, and replay-scheduling functions not yet supplied remain the memory theory's responsibility. Existing fields alone do not establish specific psychological causal relations or coefficients. A memory theory grounded primarily in emotion may reference these definitions without creating a separate emotion scale with the same name.

Current reads use the eleven-axis quantized \(y\) of [Section 15](#sec-15); no additional unquantized hidden psychological axes are output. Historical snapshots under [17.12](#sec-17-12) retain capture-time values and belong to the memory state. Historical values may be used within memory's own equations, but the relevant functions must still satisfy the admissible-input, domain-preservation, and regularity conditions of [17.9](#sec-17-9).

If memory later needs the latest committed emotion as a control input, a separate hold interface at locally finite commit points may be specified:
\[
Y_{\rm hold}(\tau_{c,k}^+)
=Q_{\rm out}(Z_E(\tau_{c,k}^{\rm read})),\qquad
\frac{dY_{\rm hold}}{d\tau_c}=0
\quad\text{between consecutive commits}.
\]
This optional future control interface is distinct from the historical envelopes already defined here. No function making it control memory is currently specified. Enabling it requires including the buffer in the joint state, initial conditions, and saved data, with the phase of every read explicit. Historical storage must not be implemented as refreshing a control input at every solver substep.

Quantized \(y\) is discontinuous at grid boundaries. Continuously driving another ODE directly with current \(y(\tau_c)\) does not automatically preserve the local Lipschitz proof. Although a hold interface makes the input constant within each interval, the commit map still requires a separate sensitivity analysis. [Appendix F.2](#sec-f-2) distinguishes uniqueness under fixed events from error/contraction guarantees. [17.14](#sec-17-14) and [Appendix F.3](#sec-f-3) further distinguish decisions based on stored values, interval flows with historical data, and current feedback.

If both modules' inputs are explicit functions of the current complete state, bidirectional ODEs can be solved as one simultaneous vector field. If an output also directly depends on the other module's input that is yet to be determined simultaneously, separate conditions for a unique algebraic-loop solution or a deterministic causal-commit scheme are required. A fixed thread execution order cannot replace a theoretical definition. Future sharing of personality or other constructs follows the semantics and update-source rules in [17.13](#sec-17-13).

<a id="sec-17-11"></a>

### 17.11 Independent Use and Joint Initialization

Used independently, the emotion module still accepts valid external memory events or receives no new replay; it need not know memory's internal \(F_M\) to solve its equations. Disconnecting the interface does not clear existing \(e,m,x,f\), sentiments, or replay-consumption ledgers; they continue evolving under the equations. Before memory-theory integration, \(T_M,r_T\) and shared-batch data are not required for independent initialization of the emotion module. The memory theory must likewise support independent use under its own explicit external-input contract. It may receive existing emotion envelopes conforming to this specification without hiding unpublished internal emotion states as prerequisites. Independent consistency does not mean absence of external inputs or shared definitions.

Joint initialization specifies admissible values at the same shared reference point:
\[
(Z_{E0},Z_{M0},\tau_{E0},\tau_{M0},r_T)
\]
along with the then-effective modes, sources/intervals, pending events, and deduplication records. Any enabled interface buffers must also be supplied. Normal memory envelopes retain the emotion subject, definition/model version, time contract, phase, source snapshot, and committed records. Historical missing values remain missing; extension fields follow [17.13](#sec-17-13). Snapshots from different effective time points cannot simply be combined into one initial state. Advancing an older snapshot requires valid inputs for that interval and its own equations. If data are missing, mark restoration unavailable; do not fabricate history.

For persistence, retain each theory's version and complete internal state, plus the joint-contract version, shared time point, time mapping, field units, owners, order, committed effects, and necessary pending data. Equal field names do not guarantee equal semantics. Any scale or contract revision must preserve this chapter's correspondences. This is a theoretical restoration interface; no memory program or real-clock connection is established at this stage.

The joint propositions in [17.9](#sec-17-9), [Appendix F.1](#sec-f-1), and [Appendix F.2](#sec-f-2) are conditional mathematical guarantees. Until the subsequent memory specification and all adapters are provided, consistency, completeness, stability, or human validity after integration cannot be claimed as proved. Existing proofs for each theory remain in place, with their applicability boundaries stated in the joint contract.

<a id="sec-17-12"></a>

### 17.12 Emotion and Time Records in Memory Envelopes

Let \(Q_{\rm out}\) be the eleven-axis output map of [Section 15](#sec-15):
\[
y=Q_{\rm out}(Z_E)\in\mathcal Y,\qquad
\mathcal Y=\{0,.001,.002,\ldots,1\}^{11},
\]
The axis order remains \((J,I,C,R,F,A,S,D,H,G,\mathrm{Fat})\). Actually outputtable values are additionally constrained by the contemporaneous long-term spatial bounds; \(\mathcal Y\) is only a larger fixed grid domain. Time fields contain time data with units, not a twelfth emotion axis, and \(t\) is not forcibly normalized to 0–1.

Define the logical memory-entry envelope as
\[
\boxed{
\mathcal R_\nu=
(\nu,\mathrm{content}_\nu,\mathcal T_\nu,\mathcal E_\nu,\mathcal X_\nu,
\mathrm{provenance}_\nu).
}
\]
\(\nu\) is the envelope-record ID. provenance separately retains the original memory_id, content version, and root evidence IDs. The same original memory may have multiple record versions with provenance. A new snapshot or version is not a new original-memory identity, new evidence, or new replay budget. The original \(B_p\) remains assigned to the original memory/concern account under [17.3](#sec-17-3). Content comes from the memory/event interface; this section does not define its internal semantic encoding.
\(\mathcal T_\nu\) stores original content time if known, the current record's effective time, and the shared-time contract;
\(\mathcal E_\nu\) stores the emotion snapshot;
\(\mathcal X_\nu\) contains the named extension fields of [17.13](#sec-17-13);
provenance retains sources, evidence IDs, recording reasons, and versions.
